**Related:** [LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md) (Snapback 3, the sibling candidate), [LLP 2000.000](./2000.000-keep-the-core-flip-two-substrates.rfc.md) (the design brief this corpus builds), [LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md) (Snapback 2, the incumbent and the control), LLP 0000, LLP 0329, LLP 0329.014.004, LLP 0329.016, LLP 1001, LLP 1002, LLP 1012, LLP 1016, LLP 1019, LLP 1020, [LLP 1023](./1023-clique-builder-jump.rfc.md), [LLP 3000.004](./3000.004-feeds-and-the-interview-reference-app.rfc.md) (feeds; Interview as the second reference application), [LLP 3000.005](./3000.005-native-jobs.rfc.md) (native jobs), [LLP 3000.006](./3000.006-explicit-data-residency-and-server-execution.rfc.md) (explicit online-only data, execution classification and synced projections), [LLP 3000.007](./3000.007-relaxed-quarry-and-explicit-residency.rfc.md) (relaxed Quarry and explicit residency: the language's constraints re-derived under the meter), `rules/RULES.md`, `rules/NOT-DOING.md`, [`benchmarks/protocol.md`](../benchmarks/protocol.md), [`benchmarks/briefs/social-messenger.md`](../benchmarks/briefs/social-messenger.md), [`benchmarks/briefs/messaging-day2-evolution.md`](../benchmarks/briefs/messaging-day2-evolution.md)

## Summary

Snapback 4 is one of two parallel candidates for the successor to Snapback 2,
and the sibling of Snapback 3, not its successor. It builds
[LLP 2000.000](./2000.000-keep-the-core-flip-two-substrates.rfc.md) as
written: the Snapback 2 core kept verbatim, two substrates flipped up front —
resources metered at runtime instead of priced statically, and a client that
replicates the viewer's declared partition instead of caching receipted
results — proved first on the hub fixture, with a deterministic simulator as
the primary harness. Snapback 3 ([LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md), led by
Codex / GPT-6 Astra) keeps the same two questions open and discovers their
answers in its first slice. Snapback 2 ([LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md))
continues as the incumbent and the control. All three are judged by one
pre-registered comparison on one clock.

This document records the author's decisions of 2026-09-12 that created the
track, restates the bet on one page so a reader can orient without the brief,
fixes the bakeoff protocol, draws the fence, and plans the first four weeks.
The design argument and its evidence stay in LLP 2000.000. Only running code
will bind (`rules/RULES.md`).

## 1. Decisions (author, 2026-09-12)

On 2026-09-12 Charlie Cheever asked whether Claude Fable 5.1 should lead a
Snapback 4 while Codex / GPT-6 Astra leads Snapback 3 and Snapback 2
continues, "and see which one turns out best." The lead answered yes with
conditions; the author accepted them ("Sounds good") and asked for this
document. The decisions below are therefore the lead's proposal as accepted
by the author. Where the author has not separately ruled, the text says so.

1. **Three tracks, one comparison.** Snapback 2 continues as the incumbent
   and the control. Snapback 3 builds LLP 2000; Snapback 4 builds LLP
   2000.000. This is a bakeoff of *bets* — discover the substrates in the
   slice versus decide them first — not of models. The result will say more
   about the bets than about the leads, and §3 is written so that it can.
2. **The name is Snapback 4.** Siblings, not a sequence: "4" does not mean
   "after 3." A separate number gives the track its own corpus root,
   directory, package identity and data directory with no rename when the
   bakeoff resolves, and files never move in LLP. If Snapback 4 loses, it is
   a number that never shipped. "Snapback 3b" was considered and rejected:
   it reads as a variant of 3, and this is a different bet.
3. **Snapback 4 builds LLP 2000.000 as written.** Its §14 open questions are
   the lead's to decide inside the track, recorded in §7 as they are
   decided. The author rules on the bar, the comparison and promotion (§3).
4. **One owner on the core.** The lead writes the engine, the store and the
   client contract. Codex lanes build the fixture generator, the reference
   applications, the device witnesses and the periphery. Blind reviews from
   two model families are reserved for the two disclosure-sensitive
   mechanisms: partition inversion of read rules (LLP 2000.000 §7.1) and
   the meter's cut behaviour on non-uniform scans (LLP 2000.000 §6). This
   departs from the standing rule that Codex codes and Claude orchestrates,
   deliberately, for the core only.
5. **The clock is four weeks** from the first commit under `snapback4/` to
   the first slice running on the hub fixture. At the clock the author kills
   the track, merges its substrates into a survivor, or continues it. There
   is no third month without a ruling.
6. **Shared and not shared.** Shared with Snapback 3: the LLP corpus, the
   Clique harness and scorers, the fixtures, the briefs, and the simulator
   once either track has one. Not shared: code, except the Snapback 2 pieces
   both are expected to copy (LLP 2000.000 §5.1).
7. **Snapback 2 keeps shipping and freezes new substrate work for the
   duration.** Fix-forward, the loop bar, acquisition and papercuts continue;
   no new certificate axes, client custody mechanisms or ceiling changes.
   This is accepted here as a condition of the bakeoff. Making it binding on
   concurrent Snapback 2 sessions needs one line in `rules/NOT-DOING.md`'s
   Moves ledger, which this document does not write.
8. **Reading load.** This root, updated in place, and one append-only
   journal, `snapback4/JOURNAL.md`. Sub-LLPs only for a decision that must
   outlive the journal. No panels beyond decision 4's two reviews.

## 2. The bet on one page

**Keep verbatim from Snapback 2** (LLP 2000.000 §5.1): the rule language
with its uniformity and hoist analysis, `decided_true` and the
structural-fact refusals; one data language, Quarry, with `--` comments,
`require … else CODE`, `shape`, `merge`, `walk`, `chunk`; the value model
and its conformance corpus; typed read and write states with no silent state
and the refusal envelope `{code, family, site, message, rewrite, guide}`; the
successor classifier (widening additive, checked narrowing, exact projection
ownership); `deliver`'s engine-computed audience, tombstones and
`bootstrap`; `schedule`, `job`, `chunk` and rehearsal; personas; `init`,
`dev`, `check`, `doctor`, `run`, `test`, `why`, `trace`, `guide`; H0
`deploy`, `rollback`, `secret`; channels; assets; the word-prefix index.

**Flip 1 — certify authorization, meter resources** (LLP 2000.000 §6). The
certificate keeps every read site's shape, uniformity, completeness class
and prediction eligibility, and stops pricing bytes and steps. A
deterministic meter charges examined rows, index entries, rule probes,
steps, heap, output and rows written as the program runs, on the server and
in the client interpreter alike. A query cut by the meter returns `capped`
with no continuation past the cut, the same observable outcome as a static
ceiling cut today; a mutation aborts with `E_BOUND` naming actual work; a
job is chunked. `check --cost` prints the structural cost class; `test`
prints measured meters on the hub fixture.

**Flip 2 — the viewer's partition is the unit of sync** (LLP 2000.000 §7).
`sync to <audience> last N by <index>` on a table makes the engine maintain,
per principal, the rows that principal may read, as a change log with a
horizon; a table without the word is online-only, and `check` says which
rule term prevented inversion. The client holds a replica of its partitions,
one watermark each, a durable outbox with client-minted ids, and the
interpreter; queries run locally and are `complete` inside the horizon.
Revocation is a scope tombstone; `until-revoked` and `delivered-history`
survive as the client's leave policy. Two client tiers — online-only
without a store, local-first with one — share one language and one server.

**The vocabulary is fixed before the mechanism** (LLP 2000.000 §8):

```ts
type Read<T> = { data: T; complete: boolean; fresh: boolean; since?: number; next?: Cursor }
            | { loading: true } | { denied: Refusal };
row.pending: boolean;
type Write = { state: "pending" } | { state: "sent"; seq: number } | { state: "failed"; why: Refusal };
type Link = "online" | "offline" | "signed-out";
```

A screen that needs a word not on that card is a defect in the mechanism,
not a gap in the card.

**The hub comes first** (LLP 2000.000 §11): the Clique generator's `u0001`
with 1,000 memberships, `u0500` with 500 follows, and about 100,000
messages are the first fixture; day-two changes are designed before the
day-one screen; the native client is in slice one; physical floors are
measured before targets; the simulator is the primary harness.

## 3. The bakeoff protocol

Shared with Snapback 3; LLP 2000 or a sibling record should adopt the same
text so that the two tracks are measured by one instrument.

**Brief.** [`benchmarks/briefs/social-messenger.md`](../benchmarks/briefs/social-messenger.md)
(Clique) M1–M8, plus
[`benchmarks/briefs/messaging-day2-evolution.md`](../benchmarks/briefs/messaging-day2-evolution.md).
The field-work application (LLP 2000 §4) tests generality after the first
slice, on whichever tracks survive the clock.

**Fixture.** The Clique generator at the instrument freeze LLP 1023 and
LLP 1024 record (`f40559f37722c79178be664a11f8a6485f1508fd`): hub `u0001`
with 100 groups and 900 DMs, `u0500` with 500 follows, and the 100,015
messages of the retained 0.0.25 replay database. The same fixture for every
track and for the control.

**Harness and judge.** [`benchmarks/protocol.md`](../benchmarks/protocol.md);
the same three scorers as the 2026-09-05 run (gpt-6-astra, grok-4.6,
muse-spark); Convex as the control arm; published packages; fresh cold
agents with only the installed package and the task. Neither lead runs the
comparison or grades their own track. The author rules on the numbers.

**Decision rule, pre-registered.**

| Gate | Threshold | How measured |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Loop bar | Fresh agent, published package, reduced Clique M1–M3, ≤ 1,200 s, ≤ 1 Snapback-attributed correction loop, N ≥ 3 | The existing loop-bar protocol (`rules/NOT-DOING.md` §"The bars") |
| Hub acquisition | Full durable content plus first offline thread and search on `u0001`, within 2× the measured physical floor | Floors recorded in the journal in week 1 (§5); the same script on every track |
| Client bytes | Online tier ≤ Convex's shipped React client; the local-first tier's JavaScript ≤ PowerSync's shipped web client, with the wasm device measured beside it and its own budget set by the diet (§7, 2026-09-13: one implementation) | Measured on one pinned toolchain, recorded once in the journal, never assumed |
| Scenarios | LLP 2000 §4's six scenarios green under the simulator on the hub fixture; LLP 2000 §10's outcomes 1–5 and LLP 2000.000 §11's 6–7 | The simulator run record plus the device witnesses |
| Interaction budgets | LLP 2000 §7's targets | Recorded beside the gates; descriptive, not gating, in slice one |
| Holistic score | Three-scorer Clique score with the Convex control | Reported; descriptive, not gating |

A track that passes every gate is a candidate for promotion. A track that
fails the loop bar or acquisition is killed at the clock unless the author
rules otherwise. A mixed result names the gains and the remaining costs, and
the author decides which substrates move into a survivor — which may be
Snapback 2 (LLP 2000.000 §13).

**Clock and checkpoints.** Week 1: floors, experience prototype, simulator
skeleton, first publish. Week 2: server core, partition stream, meter,
simulator invariants. Week 3: web replica, outbox, local queries, the chat
app on the real client. Week 4: Expo, day-two scenarios, deploy, the loop
bar, the Clique hub run, the comparison record. Four weeks from the first
commit.

## 4. Fence, numbering, routing

- **Directory.** `snapback4/`, with its own Cargo workspace and Bun
  workspace, excluded from the root workspace as `snapback2/` is. No
  Snapback 1 or Snapback 2 gate, inventory, backstop, registry or script
  applies; the pre-push hook selects none.
- **Identities.** Binary and npm shim `snapback4`; data directory
  `.snapback4/`; ephemeral ports; its own personas, credentials and process
  ownership. It never depends at runtime on Snapback 2 internals. It copies
  source deliberately, split under the 1,500-line cap on the way in.
- **Numbering.** LLPs 3000 and up; sub-LLPs `3000.NNN`; `llp-create
  --under 3000`. The 2000 series remains Snapback 3's and the 1000 series
  Snapback 2's. Design work for Snapback 4 extends this root or LLP 2000.000
  before it creates a new number.
- **Rules.** `rules/RULES.md` binds "LLPs numbered 1000 and up" by its
  letter, as LLP 2000 §9 noted for its own series. Snapback 4 adopts its
  budgets and loop shape as its own: five blocking checks in sixty seconds,
  `check` under a second, `doctor` under half a second, 1,500 lines per
  file, a bounded working set, delete rather than deprecate, only
  implemented specs bind. Its not-doing list is `snapback4/NOT-DOING.md`,
  written with the directory. The root `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md` routing line
  and the rules fence note land with the directory, not with this document.
- **Publication.** From week one, `0.0.x`, one script; the benchmark runs
  the published pin.

## 5. The first four weeks

# LLP 2000.000: Snapback 3 — keep the core, flip two substrates, start at hub scale

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Draft
**Role:** Second opinion on LLP 2000; since 2026-09-12 also the design brief Snapback 4 builds (LLP 3000)
**Systems:** Snapback 3 product architecture, bounded computation, client data model, client API vocabulary, language and tooling, effects runtime, verification (simulation), performance budgets, process
**Author:** Claude Fable 5.1, at Charlie Cheever's request
**Date:** 2026-09-12
**Revised:** 2026-09-12 (adopted as the Snapback 4 design brief by LLP 3000 decision 3); 2026-09-12 (initial)
**Related:** [LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md) (parent), [LLP 3000](./3000-snapback4.explainer.md) (the Snapback 4 root that builds this brief), LLP 0000, [LLP 0329](./0329-agent-native-rebuild-diagnosis.research.md), [LLP 0329.014.004](./0329.014.004-bounded-data-programs-v1-design.rfc.md), [LLP 0329.016](./0329.016-agentic-dev-loop-from-scratch.rfc.md), [LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md), [LLP 1001](./1001-query-engine.spec.md), [LLP 1002](./1002-dev-loop.spec.md), [LLP 1012](./1012-local-first-v1.spec.md), [LLP 1018.003](./1018.003-truthful-and-composed-client.decision.md), [LLP 1018.008](./1018.008-elegance-after-the-corrections.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.007](./1018.008.007-affordable-offline-acquisition.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.008](./1018.008.008-locality-of-application-changes.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.009](./1018.008.009-residual-ergonomic-gaps.research.md), [LLP 1019](./1019-derived-delivery-custody.rfc.md), [LLP 1020](./1020-day-two-evolution-and-legible-ceilings.rfc.md), [LLP 1023](./1023-clique-builder-jump.rfc.md), [LLP 1024](./1024-single-lane-delivered-search.rfc.md), [LLP 1025](./1025-digest-final-emission-at-hub-scale.research.md), `rules/RULES.md`, `rules/NOT-DOING.md`, `benchmarks/runs/2026-09-05-clique-full-3x3/holistic-scores.md`

## 1. Summary

LLP 2000 is right that the whole application is the unit of design, right
that ownership comes before abstraction, and right to ask for deterministic
simulation and an early real deployment. Its weakness is that it treats the
two structural choices that produced Snapback 2's costs as open research
questions, and then proposes a first experiment at exactly the scale where
those costs are invisible. This document takes a position on both, names
what to keep from Snapback 2 verbatim, and rewrites the first slice so it
can fail for the right reasons.

The position, in one paragraph. Snapback 2's *core* is excellent and should
be copied: the rule language and its uniformity analysis, the structural-fact
family, one data language, typed states with no silence, refusals that paste
a rewrite, personas, the successor classifier, the derived per-recipient
log, the loop verbs and their sub-second budgets. Two *substrates* under
that core are where the record shows the hours went, and both should be
flipped rather than re-investigated: (1) the static certificate should keep
certifying **authorization and shape** and stop pricing **bytes and steps**,
which move to a deterministic runtime meter with honest `capped`/abort
outcomes; and (2) the client should hold **a replica of the viewer's declared
partition** — the local-first shape every shipped sync engine and Snapback
2's own `deliver` log converge on — instead of a per-query result cache
proven by receipts. Those two flips dissolve most of the machinery that
leaked into the application (receipts, coverage, halos, validators, grants,
ID slices, drains, `withHistory`, follower inboxes, per-table retention
words) and turn the 28-second acquisition into a bulk stream whose floor is
the device's write speed. The first slice should be built on the **hub
fixture** (1,000 memberships, 500 follows, 100,000 messages), with the
day-two change designed before the day-one screen, a native client in the
first slice, and a deterministic simulator as the primary test harness.

Three claims this document makes that LLP 2000 does not:

1. **The costs have two named causes, and both are decisions, not
   mysteries.** Byte-priced static certification and the receipt-proven
   result cache account for every ergonomic residual in LLP 1018.008.009
   except documentation. §2 shows the mapping.
2. **The first experiment must include the hub.** Snapback 2 was "elegant on
   day one and hostile on day two" (LLP 1020) and broke at 1,000 memberships
   (LLPs 1023, 1024, 1025). A two-principal slice will pass and teach nothing.
3. **The process is part of the product.** Seventy-one LLPs, over two
   thousand commits, and seven ceiling raises in fourteen days produced a
   sound system nobody can hold in their head. Snapback 3 needs one owner on
   the core, a simulator instead of a test matrix, and budgets anchored to
   competitors so that raising one means admitting a loss.

## 2. What the record says, read as causes

The evidence LLP 2000 §2 cites is right; its reading is too gentle. Every
row below is a Snapback 2 cost recorded in the corpus, with the substrate
that produced it. Numbers are the corpus's own and retain its scope limits.

| Recorded cost | Where | Cause |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Adding `editedAt: time ?` (44 bytes × 244 retained copies = 10,736) pushed `messages.searchAll` to 4,199,688 closure bytes against 4,194,304; the builder shrank a page from 20 to 19 | LLP 1018.008.008; 0.0.21 day-two diary | **Static byte pricing.** Closure bytes are a bound on what a receipt could ship; the product paid for the bound, not the work |
| The 1,000-membership digest fold certifies at 9,922,698 heap and 8,691,704 closure bytes and is refused; the recipient-keyed form is admitted at 2,733,253 / 1,848,418 | LLP 1025 §3 | **Static byte pricing** (heap = steps × 32 + closure scratch). A runtime meter would have run the fold and reported actual bytes |
| A message page is 50, not 200, because a row "prices at 16,016 bytes" and 200 exceed the 1 MiB output ceiling | LLP 1012 §3 | **Static byte pricing** of the worst-case row width |
| A per-recipient search projection is refused at 20,863,641 reserved steps against 1,000,000 | LLP 1024 §1 | **Static step pricing** of a derivation the engine would perform incrementally |
| Full acquisition of 10,008 messages: 67.4 s → 53.6 s → 32.6 s → 28.4 s against a 2–3 s objective; 201 requests, 200 acknowledgement mutations, 612 IndexedDB transactions; PowerSync reports 10,000 rows in 0.36 s native and 2.5 s on web | LLP 1018.008.007; LLP 1018.008.009 §4 | **Result cache, not replica.** Every 50-row page is a certified query, a receipt, a durable acknowledgement mutation, and a store swap |
| Adding a count to a thread makes the composite `partial` offline whenever count authority is missing, so the template declares an independent `history` query and `withHistory` bindings; 27 files / 1,718 lines → 28 / 1,751 after the fix | LLP 1018.008 F2; LLP 1018.008.009 §2 | **Result cache.** The count is not a fact the receipt covers, so the client cannot compose locally |
| `shared/thread.ts` describes table identity, row selection, order, create membership, draft fields and mutation targets a second time | LLP 1018.008 F2 | **Result cache.** Items must be reconstructed from answers because there is no local table to read |
| Sending stalled with 85 IDs remaining against a certified 352-ID slice on an 8,192-ID grant; recovery needed a "Sync now" action | LLP 1018.008 F1 | **Result cache + engine-minted IDs.** Prediction needs server ids before commit, so ids are granted in slices |
| A follower browser tab writes through a durable inbox row; the SDK carries `forwarding`, `writer-unresponsive`, `inbox-full`, `follower` states | LLP 1018.004 | **Result cache.** One writer per partition image; other tabs cannot mutate a receipt-proven image |
| Global search over 1,000 memberships costs ~1,010 HTTP requests through a client N-way merge | LLP 1024 §2 | **Result cache** (no local index over the partition) + **static pricing** (the server-side projection is refused) |
| The complete local runtime grew 7.8 KB → 89,843 gzip bytes; the ceiling was raised 34,000 → 50,000 → 66,000 → 82,000 → 82,500 → 84,000 → 85,000 → 90,000 | LLP 1000 §"Revised"; `rules/NOT-DOING.md` §Moves; LLP 1018.008 §10 | **Result cache.** The inputs list of `metrics/twin.jsonl` is coverage spans, extent facts, held directories, posting trees, fact integrity, swap journals — receipt machinery, not the interpreter |
| `Snapshot` carries `data, next, partial, receipt{state, source, seq, next, reason, rule, sites}, freshness{live, staleSince, connection}, local{predicted, retained, caughtUp, watermark}, refusal, status, reason` | LLP 1018.003 §1 | Both. Every field is a true fact about the mechanism; almost none is a fact about the application |
| `Schema::rule_hash` binds every queued write to the complete schema; an optional column on the subject table invalidates unrelated evidence | LLP 1018.002; LLP 1018.008.008 | **Result cache.** Evidence identity exists because held answers must be re-authorized; a replica re-runs the mutation at the server and needs no evidence identity |

Against that, what scored: on the 2026-09-05 three-scorer Clique run
Snapback 2 beat Convex on authorization (82 vs 79) and offline (81 vs 44)
and lost on queries and live (60 vs 85), client integration (59 vs 79),
setup and docs (55 vs 79), diagnostics and loop (66 vs 77), operations (53
vs 76) and scale (62 vs 80); overall 66 vs 81. The later single-lane 0.0.21
and 0.0.25 scores of 82 and 85 are real improvement and not a three-scorer
comparison. The wins are the core; the losses are composition, discovery,
scale and operations — the substrates and what sits on them.

LLP 2000 §2 summarises this as "costs arise where one layer must reconstruct
another layer's facts." True, and the layer doing the reconstructing is the
client, because it was designed as a cache that may not compute. Name the
cause and the remedy follows.

## 3. Where LLP 2000 is right, and should not be diluted

- **The whole application is the unit.** §3's four properties (composition,
  locality, continuity, performance) are the right acceptance vocabulary.
  Keep them as the headings of the scenario suite.
- **Ownership before abstraction.** §5's table is the best paragraph in the
  document. §8 below turns its fourth row into a fixed vocabulary.
- **Composed reads must not fabricate.** Unknown totals stay unknown; revoked
  rows do not linger. Both flips below preserve this; they change what is
  *known*, not what is *claimed*.
- **Writes have one lifecycle with durable admission distinct from commit
  and stable operation identity.** Keep LLP 1018.004's three outcomes.
- **Rust and SQLite.** Yes, and the engine's transaction, interpreter, rule
  evaluation and successor logic are the pieces to copy first (§5).
- **Prototype the experience before the core.** Yes — and §8 makes the
  prototype's API signatures binding on the implementation, not the reverse.
- **Deterministic simulation early**, citing TigerBeetle. This is the single
  most important sentence in LLP 2000 and §11 makes it the primary harness.
- **A real deployment and a native client early.** Yes; §11 puts the native
  client in slice one.
- **Parallel, fenced, 2000-series, promotion is the author's later call.** Yes.

## 4. Where LLP 2000 is wrong or leaves the wrong things open

### 4.1 It reopens the language and leaves the bound model open; invert that

§6 asks to "evaluate Quarry" and "evaluate any alternative against the same
contracts." The one-language decision took thirty-seven paper applications,
a three-family panel, a spelling spike and an author ruling (LLP
0329.014.004 §9, LLP 1004, LLP 1007 §4b) to close, and the shipped result is
the most readable artifact in Snapback 2: the chat schema is 129 lines and a
query is three to six. Reopening the language will consume the first month.
What *should* be reopened is the bound model under it, which §6 phrases as a
comparison to "concentrate early investigation on." Half of the Quarry
refusals a builder meets are the certifier's byte and step prices wearing
Quarry syntax; the `fn`/`proc` split and the page-to-list gap (LLP 1018.008
F4) are bounded-composition restrictions the pricing model forced. Decide
the bound model (§6), keep the language, and re-derive its restrictions.

### 4.2 The first slice is at the scale where Snapback 2 looked elegant

§10 proposes "a private conversation with paginated messages and an unread
count," "two principals and two clients." Snapback 2 passed that slice in
its first week. It broke at the hub: 1,000 memberships refused the digest
fold, made global search cost a thousand requests, and refused the
envelopes builders first wrote (LLPs 1023, 1024, 1025). Every one of those
was discovered by a cold builder on a published package weeks after the
architecture was fixed. The first slice must include the hub principal from
day one (§11).

### 4.3 It investigates the client data model; the field has answered

§6 says "test a canonical local store with deliberate derived indexes,
durable pending operations, and explicit coverage and authorization
knowledge." That sentence describes a replica; the field has shipped it
under several names (buckets, mutators and a change stream, per-device
queues), Snapback 2's own LLP 1012 §6 reaches it by the hard road ("the
coverage rule that makes everything on the phone exact" is a fully drained
partition), and LLP 1019's `deliver` already computes the audience the
engine must maintain. Decide it (§7) and spend the investigation budget on
the two things the field has *not* settled for this design: partition
inversion of Quarry rules, and horizons at hub scale.

### 4.4 "Builder, human or agent" is two audiences

§3's first row conflates them. Agents need structured refusals with a

@@ -839,3 +839,53 @@ Validation uses real production restarts and interrupted replies, process driver
 faults, literal guide stage scripts, and the packed package. Credentials are
 never included in committed reports. Gate semantic gaps are handed to SEC10;
 DX10's write-advice changes only prioritize the explicit update-only answer.
+
+## 17. Wave-29 serve proofs and proof residue (DX12)
+
+DX12 extends §16's same npm script/process-group/first-JSON-line custody with
+`serve-check --then '<cmd>'` and `withServeScript(script, fn, options)`. The
+script's readiness JSON stays in memory. A command receives SNAPBACK4_SERVE_JSON,
+SNAPBACK_URL/BACKEND_URL and FRONTEND_URL; a callback receives the object and an
+AbortSignal. Neither report nor runner logs persist readiness. The shell command
+runs in the same keeper-owned group as the serve, so descendants cannot survive
+normal or deadline cleanup without a reported failure. The command's nonzero
+exit status is preserved. A successful command cannot erase a serve startup,
+lifetime, graceful shutdown or lock-release failure. Startup and proof deadlines
+are independent; a hung command names its deadline. Cleanup observes remaining
+children and the store lock before forced group reaping, as §16 requires.
+Callback failure propagates after cleanup; callback-owned resources must honor
+the signal or supply their own bounds. Arbitrary JavaScript promises cannot be
+forcibly cancelled. Escaping process groups remain outside custody.
+
+All recipes for testing an existing npm serve point to this runner, including
+probe:check, staging and day-two business assertions. Direct startServe remains
+a kit primitive for constructing an app's serve script or a disposable kit-owned
+multi-host fixture; builders need no custom npm-script driver.
+
+`renderedText` discovers reachable text through legal scroll ranges and scrolls
+a unique match into view before observation. Root overflow propagated to the
+viewport does not clip Expo web text to the non-scrolling body box. Ambiguity,
+hidden/editable text and non-scrollable clips retain their refusals. This
+supersedes §12's observation-without-scrolling behavior.
+
+`devAccounts(startDevHost, plan)` (also a DevHost method) uses the same role/count,
+provisioning mutation, service seat, custom args and role-keyed credential shape
+as production. It requires development password identity, signs up actual users
+and invokes ordinary authorized provisioning. It neither seeds tables nor
+relaxes production/staging refusal checks. Development batches are fresh;
+production roster persistence remains §16's contract and REL2's lock repair.
+
+`beginForm`'s argument object constrains its named keys: handler-owned extra
+arguments are accepted, nested named values still compare canonical JSON
+exactly, and null/undefined leaves arguments unconstrained. This supersedes
+§10's whole-object equality and fixes partial claims dropping the receipt.
+Trusted click/synchronous submit, handler claims, durable-op selection and
+actual write receipts remain required. Named value mismatches still settle an
+explicit E_WITNESS_UNCLAIMED receipt without preventing the application's write.
+
+Verification uses packed consumers for changed recipes and starters, process
+fault/deadline/status/lock tests, development real-user reads, and browser tests
+of Expo root scrolling, hidden/ambiguous text and subset/value mismatch claims.
+Wave-29 replay inputs are copied read-only from the evidence into temporary
+workspaces, measurements exclude credentials, and copies are deleted afterwards.
+Darwin/native execution is skipped in this Linux workstream.
diff --git a/llp/3000.036.004-security-invariants-and-exposure.rfc.md b/llp/3000.036.004-security-invariants-and-exposure.rfc.md
index dac0022400..43f57094fa 100644
--- a/llp/3000.036.004-security-invariants-and-exposure.rfc.md
+++ b/llp/3000.036.004-security-invariants-and-exposure.rfc.md
@@ -121,6 +121,25 @@ with the opt-in campaign-budget clarification carried into
 All transcripts are retained verbatim with provenance. Implementation
 qualification belongs to the DX11 workstream report, not these design verdicts.
 
+### 19.1 Deploy, rollback and release inspection (DX12)
+
+Apply §19's short default to deploy, rollback and releases. Completed activation
+prints verdict then REL's `live:` identity. A refused command prints the refusal
+verdict and actionable gates/refutations; derived acknowledged candidates remain
+review evidence. Default deploy uses the same `render_mode` decision filter as
+check. Advisory decisions move behind --review with a one-line count and next
+command. --verbose also retains them and expands previews; successful activation
+does not reprint its validation preview. Explicit --check keeps visibility and
+hazards with next steps; verbose expands grouped notes and storage detail.
+`releases DIR` prints compact identity/evidence counts; --json preserves its
+structured contract and --review/--verbose retains expanded inspection. JSON
+security reports keep all decisions and evidence. Gate registry, binding,
+acknowledgment and release semantics are unchanged; SEC and REL own those.
+
+Verification compares wave-29 copied schemas' deploy transcripts before/after,
+asserts verdict/live ordering, advisory opt-in and structured evidence, and
+retains a blocking-gate regression with its repair and acknowledgment commands.
+
 ## 18. Wave-26 security decisions (DX9)
 
 Implementer: Codex for Charlie Cheever, 2026-10-24. Revision 3: Muse and fresh Codex READY; implementation is in the DX9 commits. Qualification and limitations: WORKSTREAM-REPORT.md at the worktree root.

+/** Run a proof against the app's npm serve script; always stop its group and await lock release.
+ * Readiness can include credentials: keep it in memory and never log it.
+ * Callback work must honor signal or bound its own external resources.
+ * @throws callback failure, deadline, or E_SERVE_CHECK with a credential-free report. */
+export async function withServeScript<T>(script: string, fn: (ready: ServeScriptReady, signal: AbortSignal) => T | Promise<T>, options: Omit<ServeCheckOptions, "script" | "then" | "upMs">): Promise<T> {
+  let value!: T;
+  const report = await serveCheck({...options, script, then: async (ready, signal) => { value = await fn(ready, signal); }});
+  if (!report.ok) throw Object.assign(Error(`E_SERVE_CHECK: ${report.failures.map(f => `${f.code}: ${f.message}`).join("; ")}`), {report});

 }

diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/deploy.md b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/deploy.md
index 0b84b452b2..3624bcec2d 100644
--- a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/deploy.md
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/deploy.md
@@ -1,5 +1,14 @@
 # Deploy: upgrade, real accounts and schema evolution
 
+Release commands print a verdict and `live:` identity first. Advisory security
+decisions are counted in one line; `deploy --review` shows them, and `--verbose`
+expands the preview. Refusals and pending gates keep their repair commands.
+`releases DIR` is a short status; use `releases DIR --json` for machine-readable
+identity, events and paging (`guide release-json`). Test scripts against a
+running deployment with `serve-check --deployment DIR --script prod:serve --then
+'node scripts/acceptance.mjs'`; never write a serve driver (`guide testing`).
+
+
 A release is immutable source over one durable store. JSON fields, bounded ready waits and worker roots: `guide release-json`. Deploy from the app directory:
 
 ```sh

@@ -250,3 +252,13 @@ A staging clock offset belongs to its deployment store. Every CLI advance and
 redeploy prints the retained offset; rerunning against that directory keeps
 advanced business time. Use a fresh directory for an independent run, and sample
 `host.now()` rather than Date.now() for business timestamps after an advance.
+
+## Development acceptance accounts
+
+For password accounts under `startDev`, use `devAccounts(dev, plan)` or
+`dev.devAccounts(plan)`. It accepts the same `roles`, `provision`, `service`,
+`args(account)` and `timeoutMs` plan as `provisionAccounts`, and returns the same
+role-keyed arrays with email, password, principal and token. It signs up real
+password users and runs the ordinary operator mutation; rules still decide.
+It refuses staging/production hosts before creating users. Use persistent
+`provisionAccounts` for production, and `seedAccounts` for fresh staging batches.

-The same check is available to a Node driver:
+The same liveness check is available from Node:
 
 ```js
 import { serveCheck } from "snapback4/proof";
@@ -508,6 +509,31 @@ console.log(JSON.stringify(result));
 if (!result.ok) process.exitCode = 1;
 ```
 
+For any test against a running app, use `npx snapback4 serve-check --deployment production --script prod:serve --then 'node scripts/client-calls.mjs'`.
+Never write a serve driver: the runner starts the real npm script, waits for its
+first JSON line, runs the command, stops the entire group and waits for the lock.
+The command receives `SNAPBACK4_SERVE_JSON` (the complete readiness object),
+`SNAPBACK_URL`/`BACKEND_URL` and `FRONTEND_URL`. Treat readiness as credentials:
+never print it. The exit status is the command's status; a startup or shutdown
+failure makes an otherwise successful command fail. `--then-timeout-ms` defaults
+to 120000; a hung command is reported separately from missing startup JSON,
+early serve exit, children ignoring TERM and a retained store lock.
+
+For an in-process proof callback, use this complete example (set `probe:serve`
+and its deployment first). Callback work must honor its AbortSignal or bound
+its own browser/network operations; cleanup runs even when the callback throws.
+<!-- DX12 RUN SERVE BEGIN -->
+```js
+import assert from "node:assert/strict";
+import { withServeScript } from "snapback4/proof";
+await withServeScript("probe:serve", async (ready, signal) => {
+  const response = await fetch(`${ready.backendUrl}/auth/config`, { signal });
+  assert.equal(response.ok, true);
+  assert.equal((await response.json()).development, false);
+}, { deployment: "production", thenTimeoutMs: 30000 });
+```
+<!-- DX12 RUN SERVE END -->
+
 ## Production moves in a stage script
 
 Save v1/v2 sources outside snapback/. The following complete stage script

 ```
 
 With a live owner, the same moves use its authenticated local control path.

   await deploy("production", "versions/v2");

-} finally { await host.stop(); }

 ```

diff --git a/snapback4/packages/snapback4/CHANGELOG.md b/snapback4/packages/snapback4/CHANGELOG.md
index 3b951b149e..48ae3178aa 100644
--- a/snapback4/packages/snapback4/CHANGELOG.md
+++ b/snapback4/packages/snapback4/CHANGELOG.md
@@ -1,5 +1,13 @@
 # Changelog
 
+## 0.4.43
+
+### Guide changes
+
+- Test anything against an app's npm serve script with `serve-check --then '<cmd>'` or `withServeScript`; startup, command deadlines, process-group shutdown and lock release are owned by the kit. Never write a serve driver. Readiness stays private; commands receive it through environment variables.
+- Deploy, rollback and releases lead with verdict and live identity. Advisory decisions are counted; `--review` expands them and `--verbose` expands previews. JSON retains full evidence.
+- `renderedText` scrolls unique candidates into view, including Expo web document scrolling. `devAccounts` provisions real development password users with the production helper's plan and return shape. `beginForm` accepts a subset of argument constraints, retaining exact checks on named values and trusted-click custody.
+
 ## 0.4.42
 
 ### Guide changes

+  await page.goto('http://dx12.test/?witness=web');
+  await page.setContent(`<button>Save</button><script type="module">${witnessSource.replace(/export /g,'')}
+  const app={ready:Promise.resolve({ok:true}),mutate:async()=>({state:'sent',id:'real'}),client:{viewer:()=> 'user:one',onWrite:()=>()=>{},mutate:async()=>({state:'sent',id:'real'})}};
+  registerWebWitness(app,{save:{kind:'mutation',name:'save'}});
+  document.querySelector('button').onclick=()=>app.mutate({kind:'mutation',name:'save'},{id:'handler-id',title:'expected',extra:null});</script>`);
+  await page.waitForFunction(()=>window.__snapback4Witness);
+  const button=page.getByRole('button');
+  await button.evaluate(el=>window.__snapback4Witness.beginForm('save',{title:'expected'},el));await button.click();
+  assert.equal((await page.evaluate(()=>window.__snapback4Witness.formResult())).state,'sent');
+  await button.evaluate(el=>window.__snapback4Witness.beginForm('save',{title:'wrong'},el));await button.click();
+  const wrong=await page.evaluate(()=>window.__snapback4Witness.formResult());assert.equal(wrong.why.code,'E_WITNESS_UNCLAIMED');
+}));
+
+test('DX12 non-propagated body overflow still clips evidence', {timeout:30000}, async t => browser(t, async page => {
+  await page.setContent('<style>html{overflow:auto}body{height:30px;overflow:clip;margin:0}</style><div style="height:90px"></div><p>Clipped body receipt</p>');
+  assert.equal(await kit.renderedText(page,'Clipped body receipt').count(),0);
+}));
import test from 'node:test';
import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
import {mkdtempSync, mkdirSync, writeFileSync, readFileSync, rmSync, symlinkSync} from 'node:fs';
import {join, resolve} from 'node:path';
import {tmpdir} from 'node:os';
import {execFileSync} from 'node:child_process';
import {pathToFileURL} from 'node:url';
import {serveCheck, withServeScript, startDev, devAccounts, startServe, deploy, cli, command} from '../dist/proof.js';
const root=resolve(import.meta.dirname,'../../..'), bin=join(root,'target/debug/snapback4');
const env={SNAPBACK4_BIN:bin};
const schema=readFileSync(join(root,'crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/staging.md'),'utf8').match(/```quarry\n([\s\S]*?)```/)[1];
function scratch(t){const cwd=mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(),'dx12-'));t.after(()=>rmSync(cwd,{recursive:true,force:true}));return cwd;}
async function project(t){const cwd=scratch(t);mkdirSync(join(cwd,'snapback'));writeFileSync(join(cwd,'snapback/schema.q'),schema);await deploy('production','snapback',{cwd,env});return cwd;}
function script(cwd,body){writeFileSync(join(cwd,'package.json'),JSON.stringify({type:'module',scripts:{'probe:serve':'node serve.mjs'}}));writeFileSync(join(cwd,'serve.mjs'),body);}
const ready='console.log(JSON.stringify({backendUrl:"http://127.0.0.1:1",frontendUrl:"http://127.0.0.1:2",password:"PRIVATE-SERVE-SENTINEL"}));setInterval(()=>{},1000);';
const opts=cwd=>({cwd,env,script:'probe:serve',deployment:'production',timeoutMs:3000,thenTimeoutMs:3000,stopTimeoutMs:100});
test('serve callback receives private readiness, returns its value and releases the lock', {skip:process.platform==='win32'},async t=>{
 const cwd=await project(t);script(cwd,ready);
 const value=await withServeScript('probe:serve',async (ready,signal)=>{assert.equal(signal.aborted,false);assert.equal(ready.password,'PRIVATE-SERVE-SENTINEL');return 42;},opts(cwd));
 assert.equal(value,42);assert.equal((await cli(['serve','production','--wait-stopped'],{cwd,env})).code,0);
});
test('callback failure and hung callback still release serve group and lock', {skip:process.platform==='win32'},async t=>{
 const cwd=await project(t);script(cwd,ready);
 await assert.rejects(withServeScript('probe:serve',()=>{throw Error('business assertion');},opts(cwd)),/business assertion/);
 let signal;
 await assert.rejects(withServeScript('probe:serve',(_,abort)=>{signal=abort;return new Promise(()=>{});},{...opts(cwd),thenTimeoutMs:50}),/E_SERVE_THEN_TIMEOUT/);
 assert.equal(signal.aborted,true);assert.equal((await cli(['serve','production','--wait-stopped'],{cwd,env})).code,0);
});
test('then command gets readiness and nonzero status; descendants and hung commands are reaped', {skip:process.platform==='win32'},async t=>{
 const cwd=await project(t);script(cwd,ready);
 const result=await serveCheck({...opts(cwd),then:`node -e 'const s=JSON.parse(process.env.SNAPBACK4_SERVE_JSON); if(s.password!=="PRIVATE-SERVE-SENTINEL" || process.env.SNAPBACK_URL!==s.backendUrl || process.env.FRONTEND_URL!==s.frontendUrl) process.exit(9); process.exit(7)'`});
 assert.equal(result.commandCode,7);assert.ok(!JSON.stringify(result).includes('PRIVATE-SERVE-SENTINEL'));
 const hung=await serveCheck({...opts(cwd),then:'node -e "setInterval(()=>{},1000)"',thenTimeoutMs:80});
 assert.ok(hung.failures.some(f=>f.code==='E_SERVE_THEN_TIMEOUT'),JSON.stringify(hung));
 assert.equal((await cli(['serve','production','--wait-stopped'],{cwd,env})).code,0);
});
test('startup hang names script and startup deadline without exposing output', {skip:process.platform==='win32'},async t=>{
 const cwd=await project(t);script(cwd,'setInterval(()=>{},1000)');let called=false;
 const report=await serveCheck({...opts(cwd),timeoutMs:100,then:()=>{called=true;}});
 assert.equal(called,false);assert.match(report.failures.find(f=>f.code==='E_SERVE_NO_JSON').message,/probe:serve.*100 ms/);
});
test('dev accounts use real password signup and ordinary operator provisioning, and refuse serve',async t=>{
 const cwd=await project(t), options={roles:{admin:1,member:1},provision:{name:'provision'}};
 const host=await startDev({cwd,env});
 try {
  const sessions=await devAccounts(host,options);assert.equal(sessions.admin[0].kind,'user');
  for(const [role,accounts] of Object.entries(sessions))for(const account of accounts){
   const response=await fetch(host.url+'/q/myRole',{method:'POST',headers:{authorization:`Bearer ${account.token}`,'content-type':'application/json'},body:'{"args":{}}'});
   assert.equal((await response.json()).data.role,role);
  }
  assert.equal((await host.devAccounts({roles:{member:1},provision:{name:'provision'}})).member.length,1);
 }finally{await host.stop();}
 const serve=await startServe('production',{cwd,env});try{await assert.rejects(devAccounts(serve,options),/E_DEV_ACCOUNTS/);}finally{await serve.stop();}
});
test('packed CLI propagates command status and literal withServeScript guide recipe runs', {skip:process.platform==='win32'},async t=>{
 const cwd=await project(t), pack=scratch(t);
 const packed=JSON.parse(execFileSync('npm',['pack','--ignore-scripts','--json','--pack-destination',pack],{cwd:join(root,'packages/snapback4'),encoding:'utf8'}))[0];execFileSync('tar',['-xzf',join(pack,packed.filename),'-C',pack]);
 mkdirSync(join(cwd,'node_modules'));symlinkSync(join(pack,'package'),join(cwd,'node_modules/snapback4'));
 const module=pathToFileURL(join(pack,'package/dist/proof.js')).href;
 script(cwd,`import {startServe,serveUntilSignal} from ${JSON.stringify(module)}; const host=await startServe('production'); console.log(JSON.stringify({backendUrl:host.url})); await serveUntilSignal([host]);`);
 const result=await command(process.execPath,[join(pack,'package/bin/snapback4.js'),'serve-check','--deployment','production','--script','probe:serve','--then','node -e "process.exit(7)"'],{cwd,env,secret:true});
 assert.equal(result.code,7,result.stderr);assert.equal(JSON.parse(result.stdout).commandCode,7);
 const guide=readFileSync(join(root,'crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/testing.md'),'utf8');
 const block=guide.split('<!-- DX12 RUN SERVE BEGIN -->')[1].match(/```js\n([\s\S]*?)```/)[1];writeFileSync(join(cwd,'run.mjs'),block);
 const ran=await command(process.execPath,['run.mjs'],{cwd,env,secret:true});assert.equal(ran.code,0,ran.stderr);
 const installed=await import(module);assert.equal(typeof installed.withServeScript,'function');assert.equal(typeof installed.devAccounts,'function');
});

test('serve shutdown failure cannot earn success from a successful command', {skip:process.platform==='win32'},async t=>{
 const cwd=await project(t);script(cwd,'process.on("SIGTERM",()=>process.exit(7));'+ready);
 const preload=join(cwd,'npm-term.cjs');writeFileSync(preload,'if((process.argv[1]?.endsWith("npm-cli.js") || process.argv[1]?.endsWith("/npm"))) process.on("SIGTERM",()=>{});');
 const report=await serveCheck({...opts(cwd),env:{...env,NODE_OPTIONS:`--require=${preload}`},then:'true',stopTimeoutMs:3000});
 assert.equal(report.commandCode,0);assert.equal(report.ok,false,JSON.stringify(report));assert.ok(report.failures.some(f=>f.code==='E_SERVE_STOP_FAILED'),JSON.stringify(report));
});
//! DX12 changes presentation only; full evidence and refusal status survive.
use std::{fs,path::PathBuf,process::{Command,Output}};
struct App(PathBuf);
impl App {
 fn new()->Self {let p=std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("dx12-output-{}-{}",std::process::id(),{static N:std::sync::atomic::AtomicUsize=std::sync::atomic::AtomicUsize::new(0);N.fetch_add(1,std::sync::atomic::Ordering::Relaxed)}));fs::create_dir_all(p.join("snapback")).unwrap();Self(p)}
 fn write(&self,text:&str){fs::write(self.0.join("snapback/schema.q"),text).unwrap();}
 fn run(&self,args:&[&str])->Output{Command::new(env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_snapback4")).args(args).current_dir(&self.0).output().unwrap()}
 fn ok(&self,args:&[&str])->String{let r=self.run(args);assert!(r.status.success(),"{args:?}: {r:?}");String::from_utf8(r.stdout).unwrap()}
}
impl Drop for App{fn drop(&mut self){let _=fs::remove_dir_all(&self.0);}}
const SOURCE:&str="table notes:\n  title: text <=80\n  public 'fixture'\nquery list():\n  return notes first 20 by .id\n";
#[test]fn deploy_rollback_and_releases_lead_with_verdict_and_keep_live_identity(){
 let app=App::new();app.write(SOURCE);
 let first=app.ok(&["deploy","production"]);assert!(first.starts_with("deploy verdict: READY"),"{first}");assert!(first.lines().nth(1).unwrap().starts_with("live:"),"{first}");assert!(!first.contains("decision "),"{first}");
 app.write(&SOURCE.replace("<=80","<=160"));let second=app.ok(&["deploy","production"]);assert!(!second.contains("preview:"),"{second}");
 let rolled=app.ok(&["rollback","production","--to","1"]);assert!(rolled.starts_with("rollback verdict: READY"),"{rolled}");assert!(rolled.lines().nth(1).unwrap().starts_with("live:"),"{rolled}");
 let short=app.ok(&["releases","production"]);assert!(short.starts_with("releases verdict: READY"),"{short}");assert!(short.lines().count()<=4,"{short}");
 let json:serde_json::Value=serde_json::from_str(&app.ok(&["releases","production","--json"])).unwrap();assert!(json["live"].is_object());assert!(json["events"].is_array());
 let review=app.run(&["deploy","other","--review"]);assert!(review.status.success(),"{review:?}");let full=format!("{}{}",String::from_utf8_lossy(&review.stdout),String::from_utf8_lossy(&review.stderr));assert!(full.contains("decision ")||full.contains("REVIEW"),"{full}");
}
#[test]fn gates_remain_explicit_and_blocking_with_next_steps(){
 let app=App::new();app.write("table docs:\n  photo: image <=10000 ?\n  read <- allow\n  insert <- deny\n  online only\ninvariant docs always .photo != null\nmutation add(photo: image <=10000 ?):\n  return insert docs {photo}\n");
 let r=app.run(&["deploy","production"]);assert!(!r.status.success());let err=String::from_utf8_lossy(&r.stderr);assert!(err.contains("deploy verdict: REFUSED")&&err.contains("N_OPTIONAL_UPLOAD_CREATE")&&err.contains("Intended:")&&err.contains("Repair:"),"{err}");
 let json:serde_json::Value=serde_json::from_slice(&app.run(&["deploy","production","--json"]).stdout).unwrap();assert_eq!(json["ok"],false);assert!(json["security"]["securityReviews"].as_array().unwrap().iter().any(|d|d["gated"]==true&&d["acknowledged"]==false));
}

@@ -839,3 +839,53 @@ Validation uses real production restarts and interrupted replies, process driver
 faults, literal guide stage scripts, and the packed package. Credentials are
 never included in committed reports. Gate semantic gaps are handed to SEC10;
 DX10's write-advice changes only prioritize the explicit update-only answer.
+
+## 17. Wave-29 serve proofs and proof residue (DX12)
+
+DX12 extends §16's same npm script/process-group/first-JSON-line custody with
+`serve-check --then '<cmd>'` and `withServeScript(script, fn, options)`. The
+script's readiness JSON stays in memory. A command receives SNAPBACK4_SERVE_JSON,
+SNAPBACK_URL/BACKEND_URL and FRONTEND_URL; a callback receives the object and an
+AbortSignal. Neither report nor runner logs persist readiness. The shell command
+runs in the same keeper-owned group as the serve, so descendants cannot survive
+normal or deadline cleanup without a reported failure. The command's nonzero
+exit status is preserved. A successful command cannot erase a serve startup,
+lifetime, graceful shutdown or lock-release failure. Startup and proof deadlines
+are independent; a hung command names its deadline. Cleanup observes remaining
+children and the store lock before forced group reaping, as §16 requires.
+Callback failure propagates after cleanup; callback-owned resources must honor
+the signal or supply their own bounds. Arbitrary JavaScript promises cannot be
+forcibly cancelled. Escaping process groups remain outside custody.
+
+All recipes for testing an existing npm serve point to this runner, including
+probe:check, staging and day-two business assertions. Direct startServe remains
+a kit primitive for constructing an app's serve script or a disposable kit-owned
+multi-host fixture; builders need no custom npm-script driver.
+
+`renderedText` discovers reachable text through legal scroll ranges and scrolls
+a unique match into view before observation. Root overflow propagated to the
+viewport does not clip Expo web text to the non-scrolling body box. Ambiguity,
+hidden/editable text and non-scrollable clips retain their refusals. This
+supersedes §12's observation-without-scrolling behavior.
+
+`devAccounts(startDevHost, plan)` (also a DevHost method) uses the same role/count,
+provisioning mutation, service seat, custom args and role-keyed credential shape
+as production. It requires development password identity, signs up actual users
+and invokes ordinary authorized provisioning. It neither seeds tables nor
+relaxes production/staging refusal checks. Development batches are fresh;
+production roster persistence remains §16's contract and REL2's lock repair.
+
+`beginForm`'s argument object constrains its named keys: handler-owned extra
+arguments are accepted, nested named values still compare canonical JSON
+exactly, and null/undefined leaves arguments unconstrained. This supersedes
+§10's whole-object equality and fixes partial claims dropping the receipt.
+Trusted click/synchronous submit, handler claims, durable-op selection and
+actual write receipts remain required. Named value mismatches still settle an
+explicit E_WITNESS_UNCLAIMED receipt without preventing the application's write.
+
+Verification uses packed consumers for changed recipes and starters, process
+fault/deadline/status/lock tests, development real-user reads, and browser tests
+of Expo root scrolling, hidden/ambiguous text and subset/value mismatch claims.
+Wave-29 replay inputs are copied read-only from the evidence into temporary
+workspaces, measurements exclude credentials, and copies are deleted afterwards.
+Darwin/native execution is skipped in this Linux workstream.

 All transcripts are retained verbatim with provenance. Implementation
 qualification belongs to the DX11 workstream report, not these design verdicts.
 
+### 19.1 Deploy, rollback and release inspection (DX12)
+
+Apply §19's short default to deploy, rollback and releases. Completed activation
+prints verdict then REL's `live:` identity. A refused command prints the refusal
+verdict and actionable gates/refutations; derived acknowledged candidates remain
+review evidence. Default deploy uses the same `render_mode` decision filter as
+check. Advisory decisions move behind --review with a one-line count and next
+command. --verbose also retains them and expands previews; successful activation
+does not reprint its validation preview. Explicit --check keeps visibility and
+hazards with next steps; verbose expands grouped notes and storage detail.
+`releases DIR` prints compact identity/evidence counts; --json preserves its
+structured contract and --review/--verbose retains expanded inspection. JSON
+security reports keep all decisions and evidence. Gate registry, binding,
+acknowledgment and release semantics are unchanged; SEC and REL own those.
+
+Verification compares wave-29 copied schemas' deploy transcripts before/after,
+asserts verdict/live ordering, advisory opt-in and structured evidence, and
+retains a blocking-gate regression with its repair and acknowledgment commands.
+
 ## 18. Wave-26 security decisions (DX9)
 
 Implementer: Codex for Charlie Cheever, 2026-10-24. Revision 3: Muse and fresh Codex READY; implementation is in the DX9 commits. Qualification and limitations: WORKSTREAM-REPORT.md at the worktree root.

import { spawn, type ChildProcess } from "node:child_process";
import { closeSync, mkdirSync, openSync, readFileSync, writeFileSync } from "node:fs";

const children = new Set<ChildProcess>();
const privateGroups = new WeakMap<ChildProcess, number>();
const groupClosures = new WeakMap<ChildProcess, Promise<void>>();

export function register(child: ChildProcess): void {
  children.add(child);
  child.once("exit", () => children.delete(child));
  child.on("error", () => children.delete(child));
}
export interface ProofOptions {
  cwd?: string;
  env?: NodeJS.ProcessEnv;
  args?: readonly string[];
  timeoutMs?: number;
  stopTimeoutMs?: number;
  logsDir?: string;
  /** Keep stdout in memory only (stderr remains diagnostic evidence). */
  secret?: boolean;
  /** Internal: a frontend keeper owns this private process group. */

  /** Internal: the frontend observes parent death through stdin EOF. */

  /** Internal: a keeper-backed group owns npm and other descendant trees. */
  processGroup?: boolean;
}
export interface ProofHost {
  url: string;
  pid: number;

}
export function deadline(ms: number | undefined, fallback: number): number {
  const value = ms ?? fallback;
  if (!Number.isFinite(value) || value < 1) throw new RangeError("proof timeout must be positive and finite");
  return value;
}
/** @throws deadline expires; work rejection propagates. */
export async function bounded<T>(work: Promise<T>, ms: number, label: string): Promise<T> {
  let timer: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | undefined;
  try {
    return await Promise.race([work, new Promise<never>((_, reject) => {
      timer = setTimeout(() => reject(Error(`${label} timed out after ${ms} ms`)), ms);
    })]);
  } finally { clearTimeout(timer); }
}
/** Attempts every cleanup, even if a preceding step fails or stalls. */
/** @throws AggregateError after every cleanup was attempted; each step has a 10-second bound. */
export async function cleanup(...steps: Array<(() => unknown | Promise<unknown>) | undefined>): Promise<void> {
  const errors: unknown[] = [];
  for (const step of steps) if (step) {
    try { await bounded(Promise.resolve().then(step), 10_000, "proof cleanup"); }
    catch (error) { errors.push(error); }
  }
  if (errors.length) throw new AggregateError(errors, "proof cleanup failed");
}

export async function stopChild(child: ChildProcess | Pick<ProofHost, "url" | "stop">, graceMs = 5000): Promise<void> {

  const started = new Promise<void>((resolve, reject) => { startedResolve = resolve; startedReject = reject; });
  void started.catch(() => {}); // command() observes close/error instead; host startup awaits this promise.
  const didStart = () => { if (!startedSettled) { startedSettled = true; startedResolve(); } };
  const failedStart = (value: unknown) => { if (!startedSettled) { startedSettled = true; startedReject(value instanceof Error ? value : Error(String(value))); } };
  if (!grouped) didStart();
  child.on("message", message => {
    const event = message as {type?:string;pid?:number;code?:number|null;signal?:NodeJS.Signals|null;error?:string};
    if (event.type === "started" && typeof event.pid === "number") { actualPid = event.pid; didStart(); }
    if (event.type === "result") { actualCode = event.code ?? null; actualSignal = event.signal ?? null; if (event.error) error = Error(event.error); }
  });
  child.on("error", value => { error = value; failedStart(value); });
  const closed = new Promise<void>((resolve, reject) => child.once("close", () => {
    failedStart(error ?? Error("proof command exited before its process-group handshake"));
    groupGone(child).then(resolve,reject);
  }));
  // Hosts use stopChild() as their cleanup join; commands await closed directly.
  // Observe the promise here as well so an early frontend close cannot create
  // an unhandled rejection before its startup catch reaches stopChild().
  void closed.catch(() => {});
  return { child, logs, id, closed, started, pid: () => actualPid, code: () => grouped ? actualCode : child.exitCode, signal: () => grouped ? actualSignal : child.signalCode, stdout: () => options.secret ? stdout : logText(logs.stdout), error: () => error };
}
export const logText = (path: string): string => readFileSync(path, "utf8");
export async function ready(run: ReturnType<typeof logged>, parse: (line: string) => string | undefined, timeoutMs: number): Promise<string> {
  const end = performance.now() + timeoutMs;
  let outAt = 0, errAt = 0;
  while (performance.now() < end) {
    if (run.error()) throw run.error();
    if (run.child.exitCode !== null || run.child.signalCode !== null) {
      throw Error(`proof child exited before ready (${run.child.exitCode ?? run.child.signalCode}); ${run.logs.stderr}\n${logText(run.logs.stderr).slice(-4000)}`);
    }
    for (const [path, offset, stream] of [[run.logs.stdout, outAt, "out"], [run.logs.stderr, errAt, "err"]] as const) {
      const text = logText(path);
      const complete = text.lastIndexOf("\n") + 1;
      for (const line of text.slice(offset, complete).split("\n")) {
        const url = parse(stripVTControlCharacters(line).trim());
        if (url) return url;
      }
      if (stream === "out") outAt = complete; else errAt = complete;
    }
    await sleep(25);
  }
  throw Error(`proof child ready timed out after ${timeoutMs} ms; logs: ${run.logs.stdout}, ${run.logs.stderr}`);
}

@@ -1,5 +1,14 @@
 # Deploy: upgrade, real accounts and schema evolution
 
+Release commands print a verdict and `live:` identity first. Advisory security
+decisions are counted in one line; `deploy --review` shows them, and `--verbose`
+expands the preview. Refusals and pending gates keep their repair commands.
+`releases DIR` is a short status; use `releases DIR --json` for machine-readable
+identity, events and paging (`guide release-json`). Test scripts against a
+running deployment with `serve-check --deployment DIR --script prod:serve --then
+'node scripts/acceptance.mjs'`; never write a serve driver (`guide testing`).
+
+
 A release is immutable source over one durable store. JSON fields, bounded ready waits and worker roots: `guide release-json`. Deploy from the app directory:
 
 ```sh

@@ -250,3 +252,13 @@ A staging clock offset belongs to its deployment store. Every CLI advance and

+
+## Development acceptance accounts
+
+For password accounts under `startDev`, use `devAccounts(dev, plan)` or
+`dev.devAccounts(plan)`. It accepts the same `roles`, `provision`, `service`,
+`args(account)` and `timeoutMs` plan as `provisionAccounts`, and returns the same
+role-keyed arrays with email, password, principal and token. It signs up real
+password users and runs the ordinary operator mutation; rules still decide.
+It refuses staging/production hosts before creating users. Use persistent
+`provisionAccounts` for production, and `seedAccounts` for fresh staging batches.

+The same liveness check is available from Node:

 import { serveCheck } from "snapback4/proof";
@@ -508,6 +509,31 @@ console.log(JSON.stringify(result));

 ```
 
+For any test against a running app, use `npx snapback4 serve-check --deployment production --script prod:serve --then 'node scripts/client-calls.mjs'`.
+Never write a serve driver: the runner starts the real npm script, waits for its
+first JSON line, runs the command, stops the entire group and waits for the lock.
+The command receives `SNAPBACK4_SERVE_JSON` (the complete readiness object),
+`SNAPBACK_URL`/`BACKEND_URL` and `FRONTEND_URL`. Treat readiness as credentials:
+never print it. The exit status is the command's status; a startup or shutdown
+failure makes an otherwise successful command fail. `--then-timeout-ms` defaults
+to 120000; a hung command is reported separately from missing startup JSON,
+early serve exit, children ignoring TERM and a retained store lock.
+
+For an in-process proof callback, use this complete example (set `probe:serve`
+and its deployment first). Callback work must honor its AbortSignal or bound
+its own browser/network operations; cleanup runs even when the callback throws.
+<!-- DX12 RUN SERVE BEGIN -->
+```js
+import assert from "node:assert/strict";
+import { withServeScript } from "snapback4/proof";
+await withServeScript("probe:serve", async (ready, signal) => {
+  const response = await fetch(`${ready.backendUrl}/auth/config`, { signal });
+  assert.equal(response.ok, true);
+  assert.equal((await response.json()).development, false);
+}, { deployment: "production", thenTimeoutMs: 30000 });
+```
+<!-- DX12 RUN SERVE END -->
+

 ```

fn activate(
    dir: &Path,
    owner: Option<&Owner>,
    release: u64,
    previous: Option<u64>,
    revision: u64,
    bound: snapback4_lang::Bound,
    rollback: bool,
    restore_dormant: bool,
    allow_feed_adoption: bool,
    options: &Options,
    mut candidate: Option<&mut crate::release_files::Candidate>,
) -> Result<(), String> {
    let root = dir.join("releases").join(release.to_string());
    validate_registered_view(dir,&root)?;
    let digest = crate::release_files::digest(&root)?;
    if let Some(owner) = owner {
        let project = Project { root };
        let prepared = crate::native::prepare(&project, &bound).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
        crate::dev_worker::prepare_release(&project, &bound.schema)?;
        crate::release_files::link_store(&project.root)?;
        crate::release_files::durable(&project.root)?;
        let mut pointer = crate::release_files::Pointer::prepare(dir, release, previous, rollback)?;
        let activation = snapback4_core::engine::release::ReleaseActivation {
            visibility:visibility_policy(&project.root)?.or_else(||options.flags.contains_key("visible-rollback").then(Default::default)),
            release,
            expected_previous: previous,
            expected_revision: Some(revision),
            restore_dormant,
            native_digest: prepared.installed.as_ref().map(|m| m.digest.clone()),
            operation: if rollback { "rollback" } else { "deploy" }.into(),
        };
        let store = owner
            .open_store(std::time::Duration::from_secs(5))
            .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
        let engine = snapback4_core::Engine::new_release_prepared(
            store,
            bound.schema,
            bound.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program),
            &activation,
            allow_feed_adoption,
        )
        .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
        pointer.finish(engine.generation())?;
        receipt(release,engine.generation(),engine.store().adoption_report(),options, &live_identity(dir, release)?);

    } else {
        let mut result = crate::release_control::request_observed(
            dir,
            crate::release_control::Command::Activate {
                activation_protocol:crate::release_control::ACTIVATION_PROTOCOL.into(),
                release,
                expected_release: previous,
                expected_revision: revision,
                digest,
                rollback,
                visible_rollback:options.flags.contains_key("visible-rollback"),
                registry_custody:true,
                restore_dormant,
                allow_feed_adoption,
            },
            |pending| { if let Some(candidate)=candidate.as_deref_mut() {candidate.pending=pending;} },
        ).map_err(|e|if e.contains("unknown field")&&(e.contains("activation_protocol")||e.contains("registry_custody")||e.contains("visible_rollback")){format!("E_RELEASE_RUNTIME: serving runtime predates release custody; restart serve with this CLI before switching; {e}")}else{e})?;
        if let Some(summary)=options.flags.get("security-summary"){result["security"]=serde_json::from_str(summary).unwrap_or_default();}
        if let Some(view)=options.flags.get("rollback-view"){result["rollbackView"]=serde_json::from_str(view).unwrap_or_default();}
        if let Some(preview)=options.flags.get("release-preview"){result["preview"]=serde_json::from_str(preview).unwrap_or_default();}
        if let Some(origin)=options.flags.get("source-release").and_then(|v|v.parse::<u64>().ok()){result["sourceRelease"]=origin.into();}
        result["live"]=live_identity(dir,release)?;
        if options.json {println!("{}",result);} else {
            completed_verdict(options);
            print_live(&result["live"]);
            println!("release {release} is current as generation {}; {}; running serve adopted the release",result["generation"],result["adoption"].as_str().unwrap_or("adopted"));
            if let Some(origin)=options.flags.get("source-release"){println!("  retained source {origin} requalified as release {release} to register its current rollback views");}
            for fill in result["backfills"].as_array().into_iter().flatten().filter_map(|f|f.as_str()) {println!("  backfill: {fill}");}
            if options.flags.contains_key("verbose") {println!("{}",result["details"].as_str().unwrap_or(""));}
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}
fn report(verb: &str, result: Result<(), String>) -> i32 {
    // @ref LLP 3000#contended-read-serving — release completion owns no engine/lease; bound the final diagnostic drain.
    // The background writer must get a chance to emit adoption estimates before
    // this short-lived command exits. Broken/stalled output cannot hold exit.
    let _ = snapback4_core::diagnostics::drain(std::time::Duration::from_secs(3));
    match result {
        Ok(()) => 0,
        Err(error) => {
            if verb!="deploy" {eprintln!("{verb} verdict: REFUSED");}
            eprintln!("{verb}: {error}");
            1
        }
    }
}

pub fn deploy(options: &Options) -> i32 {
    match crate::store_config::StoreConfig::resolve(options) {
        Ok(Some(config)) => return report("deploy", super::configured_release::deploy(options, config)),
        Err(error) => return report("deploy", Err(error)),
        Ok(None) => {},

pub fn releases(options: &Options) -> i32 {
    report(
        "releases",
        (|| {
            let dir = existing_deployment(&directory(options)?)?;
            let after = options
                .flags
                .get("after")
                .map(|s| s.parse::<u64>().map_err(|_| "--after requires an event id"))
                .transpose()?
                .unwrap_or(0);
            let event = options
                .flags
                .get("event")
                .map(|s| s.parse::<u64>().map_err(|_| "--event requires an event id"))
                .transpose()?;
            // Read-only inspection needs no ownership handoff or source adoption.
            let mut store = snapback4_core::store_port::AnyStore::open_read_only(&dir.join("store.sqlite"))
                .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            let retained = store.dormant_inventory(None, usize::MAX).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            let mut result = store
                .release_status(
                    after,
                    options.flags.get("items-after").map(String::as_str),
                    event,
                )
                .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            if result["release"].is_null() { result["release"]=serde_json::json!(inspect_current(&dir)?); }
            result["current"]=result["release"].clone();
            result["live"]=result["release"].as_u64().map(|n|live_identity(&dir,n)).transpose()?.unwrap_or(serde_json::Value::Null);
            result["latest"]=serde_json::json!(latest(&dir).ok());
            let mut views=Vec::new();for parent in ordinary_releases(&dir)?{views.extend(super::rollback_views::manifest(&dir.join("releases").join(parent.to_string()))?);}
            result["rollbackViews"]=serde_json::to_value(views).unwrap();
            if let Some(selector)=options.positionals.get(1) {
                let n=if selector=="latest" {latest(&dir)?} else if selector=="current" {result["release"].as_u64().ok_or("no current release")?} else {selector.parse::<u64>().ok().filter(|n|*n>0).ok_or("releases selection requires N, latest or current")?};
                selectable(&dir,n)?;let root=dir.join("releases").join(n.to_string());bound_at(root.clone())?;
                result["selected"]=serde_json::json!({"release":n,"current":result["release"].as_u64()==Some(n),"digest":crate::release_files::digest(&root)?});
            }
            if options.json||options.flags.contains_key("verbose")||options.flags.contains_key("review") {println!("{}", serde_json::to_string_pretty(&result).unwrap());}
            else {
                println!("releases verdict: READY; current {}; latest {}; generation {}",result["current"],result["latest"],result["generation"]);
                print_live(&result["live"]);
                println!("{} events; {} dormant items on this page; next: releases --json for full evidence and paging; --review for details",result["events"].as_array().map_or(0,Vec::len),result["items"].as_array().map_or(0,Vec::len));
                if let Some(selected)=result.get("selected") {println!("selected: {selected}");}
            }
            super::sql::dormant_notice(super::sql::dormant_rows(&retained), "use `snapback4 sql 'SELECT …' --release <dir> --dormant` to inspect them");
            Ok(())
        })(),
    )
}

pub fn prune(options: &Options) -> i32 {
    report(
        "prune",
        (|| {
            let dir = existing_deployment(&directory(options)?)?;
            let _command = command_lock(&dir)?;
            let owner = target(&dir)?;
            let held = status(&dir, owner.as_ref(), 0, None, None)?;
            let revision = held["revision"].as_u64().unwrap_or(0);
            let confirm = options.flags.contains_key("allow-destructive");
            let result = match owner.as_ref() {
                Some(owner) => owner
                    .open_store(std::time::Duration::from_secs(5))
                    .and_then(|mut s| s.prune_dormant(confirm, Some(revision)))
                    .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?,
                None => crate::release_control::request(
                    &dir,
                    crate::release_control::Command::Prune {
                        confirm,
                        expected_revision: revision,
                    },
                )?,
            };
            println!("{}", serde_json::to_string_pretty(&result).unwrap());
            // Live responses are bounded. Enumerate the committed audit set (or
            // current preview) to name every item, never silently omit a drop.
            if owner.is_none() {
                let mut after = result["items_after"].as_str().map(str::to_owned);
                while let Some(key) = after {
                    let page = status(&dir, None, 0, Some(key), result["event"].as_u64())?;
                    let items = page["items"].as_array().ok_or("invalid inventory reply")?;
                    if items.is_empty() {
                        break;
                    }
                    println!("{}", serde_json::to_string_pretty(items).unwrap());
                    after = page["items_after"].as_str().map(str::to_owned);
                }
            }
            if !confirm {
                println!(
                    "preview only; to permanently drop this dormant storage: snapback4 prune {} --allow-destructive",
                    dir.display()
                );
            }
// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000.000#17-wave-29-serve-proofs-and-proof-residue-dx12 — scroll unique reachable candidates; hidden descendants never manufacture evidence.
import { randomUUID } from "node:crypto";
import { createRequire } from "node:module";
import type { Locator, FrameLocator, Selectors } from "playwright-core";

export const renderedSelector = `sb4rendered${randomUUID().replaceAll("-", "")}`;
const registrations = new WeakMap<Selectors, Promise<void>>();
let selectorReady: Promise<void> = Promise.resolve();
export async function registerRenderedSelector(selectors: Selectors): Promise<void> {
  let registration = registrations.get(selectors);
  if (!registration) {
    registration = selectors.register(renderedSelector, renderedTextEngine, { contentScript: true });
    registrations.set(selectors, registration);
    selectorReady = Promise.all([selectorReady, registration]).then(() => {});
    void selectorReady.catch(() => {}); // Observations await and surface this rejection.
  }
  await registration;
}

// Playwright serializes this function into its isolated selector world. Keep it
// self-contained: no application script, global callback or injected DOM marker.
function renderedTextEngine() {
  let observedText = "";
  const parent = (node: Node): Element | null => (node as Element).assignedSlot ?? node.parentElement ?? (node.getRootNode() as ShadowRoot).host ?? null;
  function textInView(node: Node): string {
    const text = node.textContent ?? "";
    if (!text) return "";
    const clips: { box: DOMRect; x: boolean; y: boolean; dx: [number, number]; dy: [number, number] }[] = [];
    let scrollCoupled = true;
    for (let element = parent(node); element; element = parent(element)) {
      const style = getComputedStyle(element);
      if (style.display === "none" || style.opacity === "0" || style.contentVisibility === "hidden") return "";
      if (style.position === "fixed" || style.position === "sticky") scrollCoupled = false;
      const clipsOverflow = style.display !== "contents" && style.display !== "inline";
      const x = clipsOverflow && style.overflowX !== "visible", y = clipsOverflow && style.overflowY !== "visible";
      // Root overflow propagates to the viewport. A non-scrolling body box
      // (Expo web) must not clip descendants that the document can scroll to.
      const rootStyle = getComputedStyle(document.documentElement);
      const bodyPropagates = rootStyle.overflowX === "visible" && rootStyle.overflowY === "visible";
      const rootOverflow = element === document.documentElement || element === document.body && bodyPropagates;
      if ((x || y) && !rootOverflow) {
        let box = element.getBoundingClientRect();
        const html = element as HTMLElement;
        let scaleX = 1, scaleY = 1;
        if (typeof html.offsetWidth === "number") {
          scaleX = html.offsetWidth ? box.width / html.offsetWidth : 1;
          scaleY = html.offsetHeight ? box.height / html.offsetHeight : 1;
          // Quirks-mode BODY.clientHeight names the viewport even when
          // this body's overflow is an ordinary element clip.
          const width = Math.min(html.clientWidth, Math.max(0, html.offsetWidth - html.clientLeft - parseFloat(style.borderRightWidth || "0")));
          const height = Math.min(html.clientHeight, Math.max(0, html.offsetHeight - html.clientTop - parseFloat(style.borderBottomWidth || "0")));
          box = new DOMRect(box.left + html.clientLeft * scaleX, box.top + html.clientTop * scaleY, width * scaleX, height * scaleY);
        }
        const scrollX = scrollCoupled && /^(auto|scroll)$/.test(style.overflowX) && html.clientWidth > 0;
        const scrollY = scrollCoupled && /^(auto|scroll)$/.test(style.overflowY) && html.clientHeight > 0;
        const maxX = Math.max(0, html.scrollWidth - html.clientWidth), maxY = Math.max(0, html.scrollHeight - html.clientHeight);
        const rtl = style.direction === "rtl";
        clips.push({ box, x, y,
          dx: scrollX ? [(html.scrollLeft - (rtl ? 0 : maxX)) * scaleX, (html.scrollLeft + (rtl ? maxX : 0)) * scaleX] : [0, 0],
          dy: scrollY ? [(html.scrollTop - maxY) * scaleY, html.scrollTop * scaleY] : [0, 0] });

    }

      const root = document.scrollingElement ?? document.documentElement;
      const style = getComputedStyle(root);
