      clips.push({box:new DOMRect(0, 0, document.documentElement.clientWidth, document.documentElement.clientHeight), x:true, y:true,
        dx:canScrollX ? [root.scrollLeft - Math.max(0,root.scrollWidth-root.clientWidth),root.scrollLeft] : [0,0],
        dy:canScrollY ? [root.scrollTop - Math.max(0,root.scrollHeight-root.clientHeight),root.scrollTop] : [0,0]});
    }
    if (!scrollCoupled) clips.push({ box: new DOMRect(0, 0, document.documentElement.clientWidth, document.documentElement.clientHeight), x: true, y: true, dx: [0, 0], dy: [0, 0] });

  }
  function queryAll(root: Document | Element | ShadowRoot, selector: string): Element[] {
    const options = JSON.parse(selector) as { source: string; flags: string; regex: boolean; exact: boolean };
    const regex = options.regex ? new RegExp(options.source, options.flags) : null;
    const needle = options.source.replace(/\s+/g, " ").trim();
    const found: Element[] = [];
    const matches = (text: string) => {
      if (!text) return false;
      if (regex) { regex.lastIndex = 0; return regex.test(text); }
      return options.exact ? text === needle : text.toLowerCase().includes(needle.toLowerCase());
    };
    const walk = (node: Node, ancestorSuppressed = false): { text: string; matched: boolean } => {
      if (node.nodeType === Node.TEXT_NODE) return { text: ancestorSuppressed ? "" : textInView(node), matched: false };
      if (node.nodeType !== Node.ELEMENT_NODE && node.nodeType !== Node.DOCUMENT_NODE && node.nodeType !== Node.DOCUMENT_FRAGMENT_NODE) return { text: "", matched: false };
      const element = node.nodeType === Node.ELEMENT_NODE ? node as HTMLElement : null;
      if (element && (/^(INPUT|TEXTAREA|SCRIPT|STYLE|NOSCRIPT|TEMPLATE)$/.test(element.tagName) || element.isContentEditable)) return { text: "", matched: false };
      const style = element ? getComputedStyle(element) : null;
      if (style?.display === "none" || style?.opacity === "0") return { text: "", matched: false };
      const suppressed = style ? style.visibility === "hidden" || style.visibility === "collapse" : ancestorSuppressed;
      const nodes = element?.shadowRoot ? element.shadowRoot.childNodes
        : element?.tagName === "SLOT" ? ((element as HTMLSlotElement).assignedNodes({flatten:true}).length ? (element as HTMLSlotElement).assignedNodes({flatten:true}) : element.childNodes) : node.childNodes;
      let text = "", descendantMatch = false;
      for (const child of nodes) {
        const result = walk(child, suppressed);
        // Block boundaries are whitespace, never concatenated words from
        // unrelated paragraph siblings. Inline RN spans retain their text.
        const block = child.nodeType === Node.ELEMENT_NODE && /^(block|flex|grid|table|list-item)/.test(getComputedStyle(child as Element).display);
        text += block ? ` ${result.text} ` : result.text;
        descendantMatch ||= result.matched;
      }
      const normalized = text.replace(/\s+/g, " ").trim();
      const box = element?.getBoundingClientRect();
      const visible = element && !suppressed && box && box.width > 0 && box.height > 0;
      const ownMatch = !!visible && !/^(HTML|BODY)$/.test(element.tagName) && matches(normalized);
      if (ownMatch && !descendantMatch) found.push(element);
      return { text, matched: ownMatch || descendantMatch };
    };
    observedText = walk(root).text.replace(/\s+/g, " ").trim();
    return found;
  }
  return { queryAll, query: (root: Document | Element | ShadowRoot, selector: string) => queryAll(root, selector)[0] ?? null,
    text: (root: Element) => { queryAll(root, '{"source":"","flags":"","regex":false,"exact":true}'); return observedText; } };
}

export function visibleText(locator: Locator, timeout: number): Promise<string> {
  // Build the function in Node, then let Playwright serialize it. Passing an
  // arrow as a string would evaluate to the function rather than invoke it.
  const reader = new Function("element", `return (${renderedTextEngine.toString()})().text(element);`) as (element: Element) => string;
  return locator.evaluate(reader, undefined, { timeout });
}

// Optional peer: CLI/process helpers still import and run when it is absent.
// Keep this graph synchronously require-able. Registration starts during import;
// withBrowser awaits that same promise before creating its context. Caller-owned
// browsers finish asynchronous launch before running a selector.
try { void registerRenderedSelector(createRequire(import.meta.url)("playwright-core").selectors).catch(() => {}); }
catch (error) { if ((error as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code !== "ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND" && (error as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code !== "MODULE_NOT_FOUND") throw error; }

// Locator construction stays synchronous for CommonJS callers. Actions wait
// for optional-peer selector registration, including when the page predates
// the proof import. Preserve synchronous Locator/FrameLocator chaining.
export const scopedLocators = new WeakSet<object>();
const locatorWrappers = new WeakMap<object, object>();
const locatorChains = new Set(["locator", "filter", "describe", "first", "last", "nth", "and", "or", "frameLocator", "contentFrame", "owner"]);
export function registeredLocator<T extends Locator | FrameLocator>(locator: T): T {
  const known = locatorWrappers.get(locator);
  if (known) return known as T;
  const wrapped = new Proxy(locator, {
    get(target, key) {
      const value = Reflect.get(target, key, target);
      if (typeof value !== "function" || key === "constructor") return value;
      if (typeof key !== "string" || key === "page" || key === "toString" || key === "_inspect" || key === "_equals") return value.bind(target);
      if (locatorChains.has(key) || key.startsWith("getBy")) return (...args: unknown[]) => {
        const next = registeredLocator(Reflect.apply(value, target, args));
        if (scopedLocators.has(wrapped) || scopedLocators.has(target)) scopedLocators.add(next);
        return next;
      };
      return (...args: unknown[]) => selectorReady.then(async () => {
        if (["count", "isVisible", "innerText", "textContent", "evaluate"].includes(key) && "count" in target) {
          const candidate = target as Locator;
          if (await candidate.count() === 1) await candidate.scrollIntoViewIfNeeded({timeout:1000});
        }
        return Reflect.apply(value, target, args);
      });
    },
  });
  locatorWrappers.set(locator, wrapped); locatorWrappers.set(wrapped, wrapped);
  return wrapped;
}

## 13. Wave 15 unsaved source advice (DAY5, 2026-10-18)

Implementer: Codex for Charlie Cheever, target 0.4.32. Empty saved-source
directories and all-empty Quarry files mean “not saved yet”. Check reports this
as information, separately from fixture drift. Missing compatibility baselines
use the same wording and still refuse regeneration; malformed nonempty saved
sources still report compile problems. No fixture is inspected against a
fabricated empty schema.

## 14. Wave 15 saved deployments and case evidence (DAY5)

Implementer: Codex for Charlie Cheever, 2026-10-18, target 0.4.32. CLI `test`
refuses when zero authored business cases execute, including when derived query
probes answer. `--fixture-only` explicitly permits fixture loading/query probing
without business cases, while actual fixture/query failures still refuse.
`testSource(..., {fixtureOnly:true})` forwards this flag. Run/skip summaries keep
business counts distinct from query sweeps.

`deploy <deployment> --save-version versions/vN` saves the actual selected .q
sources with original filenames, contemporary fixture JSONL/sweep files and a
release.json label containing actual immutable release id, source digest and CLI
version. It supports working or explicitly saved sources; saved sources use only
their contemporary fixture directory, never today's working fixtures. An
unchanged deploy labels the existing release rather than inventing a new one.
Only a new positive-numbered project-relative versions/vN directory is accepted;
the versions parent may not be a symlink. Reserve with no-replace before
activation and clean incomplete evidence on failure. A post-commit save failure
states that deployment committed and instructs an unchanged retry. --check does
not combine with saving. Existing snapshots are never replaced or filled.

Adapt previews show every rewritten/added/removed file and package changes,
including script ordering and snapback4.starter.day2. Legacy versions/v1.q and
v2.q differences from new or existing directory snapshots are reported, while
both evidence forms remain unchanged. Check identifies inherited assertion
placeholders and todo snapshots by exact shipped digest, and labels custom
npx/readiness-only proof advice heuristic. A Home starter has no todo frontend
proof or command; adapt removes only the exact recognized todo proof and its
managed commands. Custom proofs and READMEs stay application-owned. Upgrade
--quiet retains project actions and errors while suppressing successful package
manager, check and release-history output. PROD2 owns the production adaptation
action text; KIT4 owns the worked day-two proof recipe and helper API table.

Round-one repairs also cover empty flat saved files, acquisition-only residency
requiring --fixture-only because it executes no business cases, replaying both
package-manager failure streams in quiet mode, and wiring the installed DAY5
recipe into Linux and Darwin qualification. Those paths fail on its refusal;
the standalone helper alone is not qualification. LANG4 owns the remaining
compiler-note wording that recommends residency measurement without its new
explicit --fixture-only flag.

Unsaved-source classification uses the I/O error kind, not a filename/message
substring. Snapshot capture itself refuses all-empty source before reservation,
so it cannot manufacture an archive the saved-source loader would reject.

## 16. Wave-27 process checks (DX10)

Implementer: DX10, 2026-10-24, wave-27 §10b #4/#6. `serveCheck` and the packaged
`serve-check --deployment DIR --script NAME` execute `npm run --silent NAME` in
a private Unix group. One JSON object line is required; stdout stays in memory
and never appears in its report/logs because it can contain credentials. Keep
alive for 30 seconds after readiness (configurable), then group SIGTERM. Report
missing JSON, extra output, early exit, remaining live non-zombie children and
unreleased store ownership separately. Inspect group and lock before forced
cleanup, so cleanup cannot erase the failure. The keeper ignores TERM, pins the
group during inspection, and kills only its own group on driver EOF. Detached
children that deliberately leave the group remain outside supported custody.
Windows refuses; Mac execution is deferred to integration. Inspection failure, driver exit or IPC loss before authorized cleanup
rejects, never passes. Stage recipes use the ordinary live-owner control path
or exclusive cold move, and await each operation and owned-host stop.

Production account setup reuses a named local roster under the deployment,
with private fsynced atomic snapshots (file, containing directory and new directory link before effects) and an exclusive per-roster setup lock. The
startServe handle binds its deployment directory; standalone hosts must name
rosterDir. A stable role/count, mutation and service plan is required. Persist
random credentials before signup; on resumption first try login. A known
principal that cannot log in refuses instead of creating a replacement. Persist
principal, arguments and mutation ID before provisioning; replay an unfinished
mutation with that same ID under ordinary server deduplication. Completed
entries only log in, never provision again. Changed wire arguments/plans refuse; argument object key order is canonicalized recursively.
No authority bypass, grant repair or rollback promise is added. Removing or
renaming a roster explicitly requests new identities; preserve it with its
store. A stale exclusive lock requires operator inspection, never automatic
stealing. Staging seedAccounts retains its fresh-batch behavior.

Validation uses real production restarts and interrupted replies, process driver
faults, literal guide stage scripts, and the packed package. Credentials are
never included in committed reports. Gate semantic gaps are handed to SEC10;
DX10's write-advice changes only prioritize the explicit update-only answer.

## 17. Wave-29 serve proofs and proof residue (DX12)

DX12 extends §16's same npm script/process-group/first-JSON-line custody with
`serve-check --then '<cmd>'` and `withServeScript(script, fn, options)`. The
script's readiness JSON stays in memory. A command receives SNAPBACK4_SERVE_JSON,
SNAPBACK_URL/BACKEND_URL and FRONTEND_URL; a callback receives the object and an
AbortSignal. Neither report nor runner logs persist readiness. The shell command
runs in the same keeper-owned group as the serve, so descendants cannot survive
normal or deadline cleanup without a reported failure. The command's nonzero
exit status is preserved. A successful command cannot erase a serve startup,
lifetime, graceful shutdown or lock-release failure. Startup and proof deadlines
are independent; a hung command names its deadline. Cleanup observes remaining
children and the store lock before forced group reaping, as §16 requires.
Callback failure propagates after cleanup; callback-owned resources must honor
the signal or supply their own bounds. Arbitrary JavaScript promises cannot be
forcibly cancelled. Escaping process groups remain outside custody.

All recipes for testing an existing npm serve point to this runner, including
probe:check, staging and day-two business assertions. Direct startServe remains
a kit primitive for constructing an app's serve script or a disposable kit-owned
multi-host fixture; builders need no custom npm-script driver.

`renderedText` discovers reachable text through legal scroll ranges and scrolls
a unique match into view before observation. Root overflow propagated to the
viewport does not clip Expo web text to the non-scrolling body box. Ambiguity,
hidden/editable text and non-scrollable clips retain their refusals. This
supersedes §12's observation-without-scrolling behavior.

`devAccounts(startDevHost, plan)` (also a DevHost method) uses the same role/count,
provisioning mutation, service seat, custom args and role-keyed credential shape
as production. It requires development password identity, signs up actual users
and invokes ordinary authorized provisioning. It neither seeds tables nor
relaxes production/staging refusal checks. Development batches are fresh;
production roster persistence remains §16's contract and REL2's lock repair.

`beginForm`'s argument object constrains its named keys: handler-owned extra
arguments are accepted, nested named values still compare canonical JSON
exactly, and null/undefined leaves arguments unconstrained. This supersedes
§10's whole-object equality and fixes partial claims dropping the receipt.
Trusted click/synchronous submit, handler claims, durable-op selection and
actual write receipts remain required. Named value mismatches still settle an
explicit E_WITNESS_UNCLAIMED receipt without preventing the application's write.

Verification uses packed consumers for changed recipes and starters, process
fault/deadline/status/lock tests, development real-user reads, and browser tests
of Expo root scrolling, hidden/ambiguous text and subset/value mismatch claims.
Wave-29 replay inputs are copied read-only from the evidence into temporary
workspaces, measurements exclude credentials, and copies are deleted afterwards.
Darwin/native execution is skipped in this Linux workstream.
# LLP 3000.036.004: Security invariants and exposure

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Accepted (SEC design revisions 3/5 and SEC2 amendment/implementation round 5: Muse and fresh Codex READY; qualification in progress)
**Systems:** Snapback 4 Quarry authorization, check, audit, generated security tests, validation
**Author:** Codex for Charlie Cheever (SEC)
**Date:** 2026-10-18
**Revised:** 2026-10-25 (DX11 §19, Muse and fresh Codex reviewed); 2026-10-24 (DX9 wave-26 amendments; Muse and fresh Codex READY); 2026-10-24 (Integration 24 §15.1 boundary clarification, governed by LLP 3000.036.004.000 §9); 2026-10-23 (SEC7 §16 design: Muse and fresh Codex READY; implementation qualification in progress); previously 2026-10-21 (SEC5 §14 and SEC6 §15; both independently reviewed; SEC6 scoped qualification complete; code snapshot4578e98bed)
**Related:** [LLP 3000.036](./3000.036-what-the-app-farm-asks-of-the-rule-language.rfc.md), [LLP 3000.035](./3000.035-quarry-semantics-in-lean.plan.md), [LLP 3000.014](./3000.014-a-child-reads-through-its-parent.rfc.md), [LLP 3000.038](./3000.038-file-assets.rfc.md)

## 19. Wave-28 output and witness decisions (DX11)

Commissioned by Charlie Cheever, 2026-10-25. This amendment supersedes
§18's default questionnaire and exact-set family command contract. It does
not change SEC10's detector or gate semantics.

A passing ordinary `check` prints one verdict line and the existing bounded
language-enforcement summary. Only gates and reachable authored-invariant
refutations block. Advisory decisions and compiler notes require `check
--review` (also `--verbose` or JSON); the verdict counts them and names that
command. A pending gate always prints its individual command. JSON keeps all
sites, IDs, bindings, acknowledgments and evidence, regardless of display mode.

Decision identity excludes incidental locations and wall-clock campaign
results. A single canonical note set feeds JSON, display and acknowledgment.
Repeated `--ack-security ID` and comma-separated IDs with one `--reason` are
validated completely and committed atomically under the existing lock/source
race checks. An invalid member writes nothing. An audience family command
expands only to current advisory members; prior member records survive member
additions. Each new or changed member remains pending. Previously displayed
family aliases may acknowledge only their still-current original members;
never silently acknowledge a newly added member. The generated family manifest
retains each displayed family id and its individual id/binding pairs. Historical
aliases require every original member still to match; changed/deleted members
refuse the whole batch. New members are not selected. The verdict counts still
pending advisories and points to --review; their individual commands remain
there. No new member changes a previously recorded member's acknowledgment.
Batch expansion uses one captured sources/analyzer/note set and final source
revalidation under the writer lock. One invalid ID writes no entries. Unknown IDs explain that
they are stale, from a different source selection, or not decision IDs and
point to `check --review --json`. Relevant semantic dependency changes still
invalidate individual acknowledgments; unrelated declarations and comments do
not. Version-1 records remain readable; witness-bearing decisions require a
new global analyzer revision `sec11-1`, staling all older acknowledgments.
The witness-associated codes in this increment are N_APPROVAL_NOT_ENFORCED,
N_ARGUMENT_GRANT, N_INSERT_WRITE_INTENT and N_DERIVED_WRITE_AUTHORITY. Other
questions explicitly retain their unsupported/no-witness status. The candidate
is bound by its code, normalized diagnostic and semantic source dependency
slice; operation/caller/arguments/images, generated ids, sample order and
campaign timing are display/storage evidence only, never ID or binding inputs.

A concrete campaign result associated with a question is shown as a witness:
operation, caller, arguments, admitted effect, old/new image and candidate
invariant. Questions may propose additional candidate invariants to exercise
this output path: matching grant-column names to role literals, matching
approval prefixes to boolean triggers, and reusing the edit restriction already
printed by the write-advice detector. They are labeled inferred hypotheses and
never alter the gate registry, authored invariants or check exit status.
A decreasing upsert example replays two admitted writes on the engine from
the observed generated fixture; it never fabricates old/new values.
It remains evidence about an inferred specification question,
never an authored contradiction or proof. Unsupported or exhausted campaigns
say no witness was obtained, without inventing behavior. Acknowledgment
records retain the displayed witness and candidate, alongside the rationale
and semantic binding. Upsert wording explicitly says that its existing-row
arm **edits**. Derived evidence cannot be used to override a reachable authored
invariant refutation. The new witness path uses generated fixtures only, never application credential
fixtures. Schema-typed text, bytes, JSON, asset values and unknown compound
values are replaced by <redacted>; argument strings are redacted even when an
enum. Retained scalar numerics/booleans, generated principals and row ids,
source enum state names, operation names and candidate predicates describe
the behavior. History records contain sanitized arguments and state only.
A sentinel regression checks opaque redaction. Witness caches are generated
presentation evidence and never decide authority or a verdict.

Ordinary default check keeps the existing 512-work/1500ms campaign limit;
it starts no additional witness campaign. Cached concrete question evidence is
shown for gates when available. Explicit --review, --verbose, JSON and
--ack-security request a separate, deterministic, fixed 8192-work generated
question campaign (plus at most two direct concretization writes per numeric question), cached by selected source/notes and executable digest. This
opt-in budget supersedes §17.2 only for this presentation-evidence search.
Exhaustion keeps witnesses unavailable, retains every gate/refutation and
never changes an ID. A first uncached review can take seconds; default check
pays no expanded campaign. Source/fixture-authored refutations still have their
own fast campaign and complete test command. The new evidence is not a
qualification campaign and confers no proof status.

The adjoining developer-loop changes preserve the contracts of LLP
3000.000.000.001 and LLP 3000.015: refusal attribution and read-filter evidence
are developer-owned diagnostics, never new disclosure in public refusal
responses. Hooks expose `usePage.loading` and `usePage.why` as projections of
`page.read`, retaining its completeness/freshness evidence. A first-run check
hint makes the pinned React starter discoverable for projects that skipped
init, without modifying their files. Staging reports name persisted clock
offset; upload overload guidance names serialization/retry; proof docs say
text must be on screen, and verification recipes use `serve-check`. Expo's
starter offers a web file picker and native image picker.

Qualification: targeted CLI/client regressions, witnesses for wave-28 011 F1,
011 F2 and 019 F1, copies of all fifty schemas with baseline and SEC10 codes,
packed consumer snippets/starters, affected selector and reference check.
Darwin is unavailable in this Linux lane and is explicitly skipped. Finite
campaign replay is not a security proof or a promise of builder preference.
The CLI tests cover all JSON decision IDs, atomic mixed-invalid batches,
comment/independent-edit stability, historical family additions, real grant
attribution without public disclosure and authored-refutation precedence.
React lifecycle tests exercise loading, denied and stale/partial page evidence;
upload retry tests retain retry bounds and assert guidance. Packed tests run
staging/probe scripts and typecheck/export Expo's picker. Clock tests check the
printed retained offset. The 450-project new-gate census is in SEC10's lane:
DX11 introduces no gate. --review retains the compact nondecision notes and
individual questions (including the complete audience-family site list);
JSON retains original diagnostics, never an invented questionnaire ID.

requested precision fixes; [fresh Codex round 1](./reviews/3000.036.004-dx11-codex-r1-20261025.md)
was READY. [Muse round 2](./reviews/3000.036.004-dx11-muse-r2-20261025.md) and
[fresh Codex round 2](./reviews/3000.036.004-dx11-codex-r2-20261025.md) were READY

All transcripts are retained verbatim with provenance. Implementation

### 19.1 Deploy, rollback and release inspection (DX12)

Apply §19's short default to deploy, rollback and releases. Completed activation
prints verdict then REL's `live:` identity. A refused command prints the refusal
verdict and actionable gates/refutations; derived acknowledged candidates remain
review evidence. Default deploy uses the same `render_mode` decision filter as
check. Advisory decisions move behind --review with a one-line count and next
//! Operator preflight runs the real activation on a private disk-backed snapshot.
// REL3 owns dormant-visibility counts here; SURF clause narration lives in release_narration.
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#92-exact-preview-and-operator-vocabulary — no live pointer, generation or audit changes.
use super::*;
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
struct Snapshot(PathBuf);
impl Drop for Snapshot {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&self.0);
    }
}

pub(super) fn plan(
    store_path: &Path,
    root: PathBuf,
    release: u64,
    options: &Options,
    held_owner: Option<&crate::store_owner::Owner>,
) -> Result<serde_json::Value, String> {
    if let Some(path)=options.flags.get("bridge") {
        let dir=store_path.parent().ok_or("no deployment directory")?;
        let mut store=snapback4_core::store_port::AnyStore::open_read_only(store_path).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
        let current=store.release_status(0,None,None).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?["release"].as_u64().ok_or("a bridge needs a current release")?;
        super::release_bridge::write(Path::new(path),dir,current,release,&[])?;
        eprintln!("bridge starter written to {path}; follow ROLLBACK-CHECKLIST.md before selecting exact source");
    }
    let bound = super::releases::bound_at(root.clone())?;
    let project = Project { root };
    let native = crate::native::prepare(&project, &bound).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    crate::dev_worker::prepare_release(&project,&bound.schema)?;
    let id = snapback4_core::ids::mint(snapback4_core::now_ms(), &mut snapback4_core::ids::Mint::default())
        .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    let snapshot = Snapshot(std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("snapback4-release-preview-{id}")));
    snapback4_core::private_fs::directory(&snapshot.0).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    let path = snapshot.0.join("store.sqlite");
    // Probe the existing filesystem lock, independently of any HTTP address
    // hint. Keep a stopped store excluded through preview; never create a lock
    // sidecar or mutate its address record just to inspect ownership.
    let (live,_stopped_guard)=if held_owner.is_some() {(false,None)} else if store_path.exists() {
        let database=store_path.canonicalize().map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
        match snapback4_core::private_fs::auxiliary_file(&database,&snapback4_core::private_fs::owner_path(&database),false,0) {
            Ok(owner)=>match owner.try_lock() {
                Ok(())=>(false,Some(owner)),
                Err(std::fs::TryLockError::WouldBlock)=>(true,None),
                Err(std::fs::TryLockError::Error(e))=>return Err(format!("preview owner probe failed: {e}")),
            },
            Err(e) if e.kind()==std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound=>return Err("preview cannot establish exclusive store ownership: missing owner sidecar; restart serve once, stop it, then retry".into()),
            Err(e)=>return Err(format!("preview owner probe failed: {e}")),
        }
    }else{(false,None)};
    // SQLite VACUUM INTO takes a coherent read snapshot even with a live WAL writer.
    // Busy/disk errors refuse this preview; never fall back to copying database bytes.
    if store_path.exists() {
    let source = snapback4_core::store_port::AnyStore::open_read_only(store_path).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    source
        .snapshot_to(&path)
        .map_err(|e| {
            format!("snapshot preview failed (retry after store/disk becomes available): {e}")
        })?;
    drop(source);
    }
    let mut store = snapback4_core::store_port::AnyStore::open(&path).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    store.preview_asset_root(&store_path.parent().ok_or("preview needs a deployment directory")?.join("assets")).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
    let held = store
        .release_status(0, None, None)
        .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    // @ref LLP 3000.000.000.002#4-staging-clock — preview uses the owning host's authoritative time, without minting or advancing anything.
    let activation_now=if live {
        let status=crate::release_control::request(store_path.parent().ok_or("preview store has no deployment directory")?,crate::release_control::Command::Status{after:0,items_after:None,event:None})?;
        if status["revision"]!=held["revision"] {return Err("preview raced release activation; retry against the current store".into());}
        status["clock"]["now"].as_i64().ok_or("serving owner does not expose its activation clock; restart it with this CLI before preview")?
    }else{snapback4_core::now_ms()};
    let mut activation =
        snapback4_core::engine::release::ReleaseActivation::new(release, held["release"].as_u64());
    activation.visibility=super::releases::visibility_policy(&project.root)?.or_else(||options.flags.contains_key("visible-rollback").then(Default::default));
    activation.expected_revision = held["revision"].as_u64();
    activation.native_digest = native.installed.map(|n| n.digest);
    activation.operation = "preview".into();
    activation.restore_dormant = !options.flags.contains_key("deploy-direction") || options.flags.contains_key("restore-dormant");
    let mut steps = Vec::new();
    let estimate = store
        .adoption_estimate(&bound.schema)
        .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?
        .map(|e| e.summary());
    let mut client_compatibility=Vec::new();
    if let Some(current)=held["release"].as_u64() {
        let prior=super::releases::bound_at(store_path.parent().ok_or("no deployment directory")?.join("releases").join(current.to_string()))?;
        client_compatibility=snapback4_lang::client_contracts::compare(&prior,&bound,&format!("release {current}"));
    }
    if let Some(advice)=options.flags.get("client-baseline-advice"){client_compatibility.extend(serde_json::from_str::<Vec<String>>(advice).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?);}
    let candidate_schema=bound.schema.clone();
    let candidate_programs:std::collections::BTreeMap<_,_>=bound.programs.iter().map(|p|(p.program.name.clone(),p.program.clone())).collect();
    let mut hazards=Vec::new();
    let mut notes=Vec::new();
    let prior:Option<(String,String)>=store.inspection_generation().ok();
    let prior_schema=prior.as_ref().map(|(schema,_)|serde_json::from_str::<snapback4_core::Schema>(schema).map_err(|e|e.to_string())).transpose()?;
    if let Some((schema,programs))=prior {
        let prior:snapback4_core::Schema=serde_json::from_str(&schema).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
        let table_names=store.release_table_mapping(&prior,&candidate_schema)?;
        steps=snapback4_core::successor::classify(&prior.with_table_names(&table_names).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?,&candidate_schema);
        for (old,new) in &table_names {notes.push(format!("table {old} → {new}: the same retained identity and references; review operations and rules at the target spelling"));}
        let programs:std::collections::BTreeMap<String,snapback4_core::ir::Program>=serde_json::from_str(&programs).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
        let target_label = format!("release {release}");
        let compatibility = super::release_narration::compatibility(&store, &prior, &candidate_schema, held["release"].as_u64().unwrap_or(0), &target_label, options.flags.contains_key("deploy-direction"))?;
        for line in compatibility {
            if let Some(warning) = line.strip_prefix("WARNING ") { hazards.push(warning.to_owned()); }
            else { notes.push(line.trim_start_matches("note: ").to_owned()); }
        }
        for (name,program) in &candidate_programs {
            if !programs.contains_key(name) {continue;}
            for (table,_) in snapback4_core::ir::write_tables(program) {
                if prior.table(&table).is_none() && !table_names.values().any(|new|new==&table) && candidate_schema.maintain_for(&table).is_none() {
                    hazards.push(format!("operation {name} remains callable by old clients but now writes newer-only table {table}; the old target has no read path for these records; declare a table rename for one identity, or retain the table and adapt the old read operation in a bridge"));
                }
            }
        }
        let rollback=!options.flags.contains_key("deploy-direction");
        let (behavior_warnings, behavior_notes) = super::release_narration::behavior(store_path, &store, &prior, &candidate_schema, &programs, &candidate_programs, &project, held["release"].as_u64(), &target_label, rollback)?;
        hazards.extend(behavior_warnings); notes.extend(behavior_notes);
        for name in prior.tables.keys().filter(|name|!candidate_schema.tables.contains_key(*name) && !table_names.contains_key(*name) && prior.maintain_for(name).is_none() && prior.tables[*name].ephemeral.is_none()) {
            let count:u64=store.inspection_table_population(name)?;
            if count>0 {hazards.push(format!("table {name}: {count} rows become dormant; {target_label} lacks this table's behavior; preserve it in a bridge or accept its absence"));}
        }
    }
    super::release_safeguards::capture(&store)?;
    let mut engine = snapback4_core::Engine::new_release_prepared_at(
        store,
        bound.schema,
        bound.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program),
        &activation,
        options.flags.contains_key("allow-feed-adoption"),
        activation_now,
    )
    .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    let mut view_effects=Vec::new();
    let rollback_views=super::rollback_views::manifest(&project.root)?;
    for view in &rollback_views {
        let prepared=project.root.join("rollback-views").join(view.release.to_string());
        let root=if prepared.is_dir(){prepared}else{store_path.parent().unwrap().join("releases").join(view.release.to_string())};
        let target=super::releases::bound_at(root.clone())?;
        let path=snapshot.0.join(format!("view-{}.sqlite",view.release));
        engine.store().snapshot_to(&path).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
        let mut store=snapback4_core::store_port::AnyStore::open(&path).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
        store.preview_asset_root(&store_path.parent().unwrap().join("assets")).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
        super::release_safeguards::capture(&store)?;
        let mut selection=snapback4_core::engine::release::ReleaseActivation::new(view.release,Some(release));
        selection.visibility=super::releases::visibility_policy(&root)?;selection.operation="preview".into();selection.restore_dormant=true;
        let target_schema=target.schema.clone();
        let selected=snapback4_core::Engine::new_release_prepared_at(store,target.schema,target.programs.into_iter().map(|p|p.program),&selection,options.flags.contains_key("allow-feed-adoption"),activation_now).map_err(|e|format!("rollback view for release {} refuses: {e}",view.target))?;
        let visible=super::release_visibility::assess(selected.store(),Some(&candidate_schema),&target_schema)?;
        if visible.hidden_rows>0&&view.hides.is_empty(){return Err(format!("E_ADOPT: rollback view for release {} still hides {} old-table rows; declare a truthful down or intentional hiding",view.target,visible.hidden_rows))}
        view_effects.push(serde_json::json!({"target":view.target,"view":view.release,"visibility":visible}));
        notes.push(format!("rollback to release {} via bridge {}: {} hidden old-table rows; {} newer-only rows; {}",view.target,view.release,visible.hidden_rows,visible.newer_only_rows,view.hides.join("; ")));
    }
    let mut planned = engine
        .store_mut()
        .release_status(0, None, None)
        .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    planned["items"] = serde_json::to_value(
        engine
            .store_mut()
            .dormant_inventory(None, usize::MAX)
            .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?,
    )
    .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    let items: Vec<snapback4_core::store_port::DormantItem> = serde_json::from_value(planned["items"].clone()).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    let visibility = super::release_visibility::assess(engine.store(), prior_schema.as_ref(), &candidate_schema)?;
    let mut rollback_visibility = None;
    let mut effects = super::release_safeguards::warnings(engine.store(),prior_schema.as_ref(),&candidate_schema,&candidate_programs,&items,release,!options.flags.contains_key("deploy-direction"))?;
    effects.append(&mut hazards); hazards=effects;
    for fill in &engine.store().adoption_report().backfills { notes.push(format!("backfill: {fill}; confirm these historical values before selecting release {release}")); }
    steps.retain(|step|!step.message.contains("engine capture"));
    for step in &mut steps {
        if step.change==snapback4_core::successor::Change::Narrowing {
            step.change=snapback4_core::successor::Change::Additive;
            step.message=format!("{}; active rows satisfy the candidate after dormant projection",step.message.split(';').next().unwrap_or(&step.message));
        }
        if let Some(position)=step.path.strip_prefix("maintains[").and_then(|s|s.strip_suffix(']')).and_then(|s|s.parse::<usize>().ok()) {
            let label=candidate_schema.maintains[position].label();
            let visits=engine.store().adoption_report().views.iter().find(|view|view.name==label).map(|v|v.rows).unwrap_or(0);
            step.message=format!("maintain {label}: rebuilt from {visits} source rows");
            step.change=snapback4_core::successor::Change::Additive;
        }
        let path=step.path.strip_prefix("tables.").unwrap_or(&step.path).replace(".columns.",".");
        let count=planned["items"].as_array().into_iter().flatten().find(|i|i["path"].as_str()==Some(path.as_str())).and_then(|i|i["rows"].as_u64()).unwrap_or(0);
        let unit = if step.path.contains(".columns.") { "cells" } else if step.path.contains(".indexes.") { "entries" } else { "rows" };
        // @ref LLP 3000.000.000#11-wave-11-factual-release-narration — per-path prose uses the same disjoint counts as the inventory totals.
        let retained = if count == 0 && unit == "cells" {
            format!("no standalone {unit} become dormant (cells of any dormant rows are counted with those rows)")
        } else if count == 0 {
            format!("no {unit} become dormant")
        } else {
            format!("its {unit} become dormant ({count} {unit}; restored on forward)")
        };
        step.message=step.message.replace("its rows are lost", &retained).replace("its values are lost", &retained);
    }
    if options.flags.contains_key("deploy-direction") {
        if let Some(current) = held["release"].as_u64() {
            let old_root=store_path.parent().ok_or("no deployment directory")?.join("releases").join(current.to_string());
            let old=super::releases::bound_at(old_root.clone())?;
            let covered=rollback_views.iter().find(|v|v.target==current);
            let risks=super::rollback_foresight::risks(&candidate_schema,&old.schema,current,store_path.parent().unwrap());
            if let Some(view)=covered {for risk in risks {notes.push(format!("{risk}; default rollback covered by registered view {} (exact source remains subject to this risk)",view.release));}}
            else {hazards.extend(risks);}
            // Exact reverse adoption tests today's post-deploy image, separately from future-write risks.
            let reverse_path=snapshot.0.join("reverse.sqlite");
            engine.store().snapshot_to(&reverse_path).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
            let mut reverse_store=snapback4_core::store_port::AnyStore::open(&reverse_path).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
            reverse_store.preview_asset_root(&store_path.parent().unwrap().join("assets")).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
            super::release_safeguards::capture(&reverse_store)?;
            let native=crate::native::prepare(&Project {root:old_root},&old).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
            let mut reverse=snapback4_core::engine::release::ReleaseActivation::new(current,Some(release));
            reverse.restore_dormant=true;
            reverse.native_digest=native.installed.map(|m|m.digest);
            reverse.operation="preview".into();
            let old_schema=old.schema.clone();
            match snapback4_core::Engine::new_release_prepared_at(reverse_store,old.schema,old.programs.into_iter().map(|p|p.program),&reverse,options.flags.contains_key("allow-feed-adoption"),activation_now) {
                Ok(reverse_engine) => {
                    let effects=super::release_visibility::assess(reverse_engine.store(),Some(&candidate_schema),&old_schema)?;
                    if effects.hidden_rows>0 || effects.empty_cells>0 || effects.newer_only_rows>0 {
                        for line in effects.lines(release,current) { hazards.push(format!("rollback foresight: {}",line.trim())); }
                    }
                    if effects.hidden_rows>0&&!rollback_views.iter().any(|v|v.target==current){return Err(format!("E_ADOPT: deploy would hide {} old-table rows on rollback to release {current}; retain a compiled guarded bridge or declare intentional hiding",effects.hidden_rows))}
                    rollback_visibility=Some(effects);
                },
                Err(error) if covered.is_some()=>notes.push(format!("exact rollback to release {current} refuses: {error}; default rollback covered by registered view {}",covered.unwrap().release)),
                Err(error) => hazards.push(format!("rollback to release {current} refuses on the post-deploy snapshot: {error}; run `snapback4 rollback {} --to {current} --check --bridge <new-dir>` and preserve the required fields/domains in a bridge",store_path.parent().unwrap().display())),
            }
        }
    }
    hazards.extend(client_compatibility.iter().filter(|line|!snapback4_lang::client_contracts::is_note(line)).cloned());
    notes.extend(client_compatibility.iter().filter(|line|snapback4_lang::client_contracts::is_note(line)).cloned());
    let checklist = super::release_bridge::checklist(held["release"].as_u64().unwrap_or(0),release,&hazards);
    let payload=serde_json::json!({"sourceRelease":options.flags.get("source-release").and_then(|s|s.parse::<u64>().ok()),"preview":true,"security":options.flags.get("security-summary").and_then(|s|serde_json::from_str::<serde_json::Value>(s).ok()),"visibility":visibility,"rollbackVisibility":rollback_visibility,"rollbackViews":rollback_views,"rollbackViewEffects":view_effects,"rollbackView":options.flags.get("rollback-view").and_then(|s|serde_json::from_str::<serde_json::Value>(s).ok()),"clientCompatibility":client_compatibility,"checklist":checklist,"hazards":hazards,"notes":notes,"current":held,"plan":planned,"steps":steps,"estimate":estimate,"phases":engine.store().adoption_report().summary(),"backfills":engine.store().adoption_report().backfills});
    if !options.json&&options.flags.contains_key("deploy-direction") && !options.flags.contains_key("validation-only") {
        let hidden=view_effects.iter().filter_map(|v|v["visibility"]["hiddenRows"].as_u64()).sum::<u64>();
        println!("deploy verdict: READY; {} rollback views, {hidden} hidden old-table rows across those views; {}",rollback_views.len(),super::security::release_verdict(options.flags.get("security-summary")));
    }
    if options.flags.contains_key("validation-only") {
        if !options.json && options.flags.contains_key("verbose") {
            if !options.flags.contains_key("deploy-direction") {for line in visibility.lines(held["release"].as_u64().unwrap_or(0),release) {eprintln!("{line}");}}
            super::release_summary::print(prior_schema.as_ref(),&candidate_schema,&candidate_programs,&snapback4_lang::client_contracts::compact(&hazards),&notes,&steps,&items);
        }
        return Ok(payload);
    }

    if options.json {
        println!("{}",serde_json::to_string_pretty(&payload).unwrap());
    } else {
        if !options.flags.contains_key("deploy-direction") {
            for line in visibility.lines(held["release"].as_u64().unwrap_or(0),release) {println!("{line}");}
        }
        if !options.flags.contains_key("deploy-direction") {println!("rollback verdict: READY; preview only; live release unchanged");}
        println!("preview: release {release} would be current as generation {}; live release unchanged", engine.generation());
        if options.flags.contains_key("verbose") {
            super::release_summary::print(prior_schema.as_ref(), &candidate_schema, &candidate_programs, &snapback4_lang::client_contracts::compact(&hazards), &notes, &steps, &items);
        } else {
            for hazard in snapback4_lang::client_contracts::compact(&hazards) {println!("  WARNING {hazard}; next: prove the affected cases before activation; --verbose for the full preview");}
        }
        if !options.flags.contains_key("deploy-direction") { println!("  checklist: prove the named refusal cases and affected lists/counts as real accounts before release {release}; `--bridge <new-dir>` saves loosened target source plus this app's ROLLBACK-CHECKLIST.md"); }
